[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 6.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b2bb66","wall_time_seconds":2.9448307,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":42,"output":"\\r\\nName\\r\\n----\\r\\nprepare-assign.mjs\\r\\nlaunch_lsatu_browser.py\\r\\nlsatu\\r\\nlsatu_api.py\\r\\nlsatu-admin.mjs\\r\\nlsatu-lesson-mcp.mjs\\r\\nlsatu-lesson-validate.mjs\\r\\nlsatu-report.mjs\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ae683f","wall_time_seconds":3.2028556,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":22133,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 22133)\\nTotal output lines: 147\\n\\n12:      \\"Must Be True\\"\\r\\n22:      \\"left_md\\": \\"Within the world of Inference (**Most Supported / Must Be True**), we are trying to combine 2 or more ideas together in order to derive some synthesis of ideas.\\\\n\\\\nWe often can\'t predict what the correct answer will say, but we often can, if we pay attention to certain \\\\\\"clue words\\\\\\":\\\\n\\\\n- conditional indicators (if, only if, unless, requires, etc.)\\\\n- causal relationships (because of this, due to this, this allows, this makes possible, as a result, this leads to, etc.)\\\\n- quantifiers (most, some, all, no)\\\\n- other mathematical ideas (percents, fractions, numbers, ratios)\\\\n- pivots (but, yet, however)\\\\n\\\\nThe pattern we call _Reconcile the Pivot_ is when the last claim begins with a Pivot word. That provides a strong clue that we\'re supposed to reconcile that final fact with what came before it.\\",\\r\\n28:        \\"Must Be True\\"\\r\\n68:      \\"left_md\\": \\"# Parallel and Paradox\\\\n\\\\ngo together like ketchup and soup\\\\n\\\\nWhich is to say, .... _not at all!_\\\\n\\\\nWe\'re purposefully mixing oil and water in this question set to remind our brain of how it feels on a real Logical Reasoning section when you have to totally take off one brain and put on another one, as you switch between some question types.\\\\n\\\\nCertainly, there\'s not much switching cost involved when you switch within a family.\\\\n\\\\nReading a stimulus for Strengthen, Weaken, Evaluate, Flaw, and Necessary Assumption will feel pretty similar. Reading an Agree / Disagree and a Most Supported feel somewhat the same.\\\\n\\\\nBut Parallel and Paradox are pretty darn far apart. Why?\\\\n\\\\n- What are we reading in each case. Is the stimulus an argument or not? Is it likely to have conditional logic or not?\\\\n\\\\n- How are we judging answers: based on whether they can be inferred? based on whether they would have the desired impact, if true? based on whether we can match them up with the stimulus?\\",\\r\\n69:      \\"right_md\\": \\"# Try these 6 questions in 9 minutes\\\\n\\\\n(1.5x = 14 mins, 2x = 18 mins)  \\\\nParallel tends to take longer than Paradox, so pace accordingly\\\\n\\\\n## PARADOX\\\\n\\\\n- We\'re not reading an argument.\\\\n- The question stem will tell us what surprising idea we need to explain, or the final sentence of the stimulus will use a pivot word like \\\\\\"but, yet, however\\\\\\" to show us the surprising idea\\\\n- Figure out what background fact, if any, makes this idea a surprise. Frame your thinking like this:\\\\n\\\\n_Given that .. background fact(s),_  \\\\n_How can we explain ... surprising claim?_\\\\n\\\\n## PARALLEL\\\\n\\\\n- We are reading an argument.\\\\n- Our job is to see the structure or reasoning pattern behind the argument.\\\\n- If it\'s possible to describe the reasoning pattern conversationally (e.g. \\\\\\"The author is arguing that if the extra effort has diminishing returns, it ain\'t worth it\\\\\\") go for it.\\\\n- But if we see _Conditionals, Quantifiers, or Repeating Ideas_, then it\'s usually better to create a little algebraic recipe for the argument.\\\\n- Once you have a recipe for the argument, articulate the _first_ ingredient you plan to look for. Are you looking to see if there are two \\\\\\"most\\\\\\" claims? Are you checking to see whether the Conclusion has the same _probably_ strength as the original? Are you seeing whether there are two conditional premises that chain together?\\\\n- Be suspicious of answers that have similar topics or other cheap superficial similarities.\\"\\r\\n113:      \\"left_md\\": \\"## This set has 7 **Principle-Strengthen**\\\\n## questions from tests 55-59\\\\n\\\\nAll of these examples are the much-more common style of Principle-Strengthen in which the _answers_ are principles. They often feel more like **Sufficient Assumption** than Strengthen, because the correct answer is usually a conditional rule that is triggered by the Evidence and results in the Conclusion.\\\\n\\\\nBut the question stem is technically a Strengthen task (which answer _most_ justifies), so we\'re not supposed to ask ourselves, \\\\\\"Does this answer prove the conclusion\\\\\\", even though in many cases it will. In some rare cases, though, the correct answer could be as weakly worded as a Necessary Assumption.\\\\n\\\\nNevertheless, our primary approach to these is to expect a conditional logic answer, so we pre-phrase by articulating an \\\\\\"IF premise, THEN conclusion\\\\\\" reasoning move.\\\\n\\\\nMost of the answers will be conditionals. If so, we want to be very **stubborn with the outcome** . The right side of the rule should match the conclusion (or at least sound like wording that would strongly support the conclusion). We want to be **flexible with the trigger** .\\\\n\\\\nOther answers feel more like comparative ideas that help _Weigh Tradeoffs_ or establish priorities. When arguments seem to be balancing competing ideas (a pro and a con / this obligation with that obligation), then when the author issues her judgment in the conclusion, she\'s revealing whether she cares more about the good thing or the bad thing, whether she cares more about this obligation or that obligation.\\\\n\\\\nGiven this argument, we\'d anticipate a conditional answer\\\\n_Billy lied to our customers. Thus he shouldn\'t be on our staff anymore._\\\\n\\\\n(A) A person can be on the staff only if they are consistently truthful with customers\\\\n\\\\nGiven this argument, we might see a weighing-tradeoffs answer\\\\n_Billy is extremely dependable, although she lied to our customers. Clearly, she shouldn\'t be on our staff._\\\\n\\\\n(A) Dependability is a less important trait in a staff member than is honesty.\\",\\r\\n114:      \\"right_md\\": \\"## Try these 7 problems, **untimed**\\\\n\\\\n- Once you find the Conclusion and identify the main Supporting Idea(s), you will generally want to articulate an, \\\\\\"If [premise], then [conclusion]\\\\\\" linking idea.\\\\n\\\\n- If, otherwise, you felt like the argument was grappling with competing interests, then articulate how the author is weighing tradeoffs:\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\\\"she cares more about X than about Y\\\\\\".\\\\n\\\\n- Try to get rid of a lot answers quickly because they don\'t have any good matching language for the conclusion, or because the matching language for the conclusion is being put on the **left** of the conditional arrow.\\\\n\\\\n- Correct answers will often, if not usually, be written in contrapositive form, so be willing to try that move. If you read \\\\\\"should be on staff only if consistently truthful\\\\\\", then you have to make your brain say, \\\\\\"Okay, this is saying _if you\'re_ **_not_** _consistently truthful, should_ **_not_** _on staff_ \\\\\\".\\"\\r\\n187:      \\"Must Be True\\"\\r\\n197:      \\"left_md\\": \\"Within the world of Inference (**Most Supported / Must Be True**), we are trying to combine 2 or more ideas together in order to derive some synthesis of ideas.\\\\n\\\\nWe often can\'t predict what the correct answer will say, but we often can, if we pay attention to certain \\\\\\"clue words\\\\\\":\\\\n\\\\n- conditional indicators (if, only if, unless, requires, etc.)\\\\n- causal relationships (because of this, due to this, this allows, this makes possible, as a result, this leads to, etc.)\\\\n- quantifiers (most, some, all, no)\\\\n- other mathematical ideas (p…18633 tokens truncated…n two things being treated similarly? Is there something sketchy about the author\'s evidence or her reasoning?\\\\n\\\\nHow do we know which one we\'re doing? We don\'t, really.\\\\n\\\\n**Sufficient Assumption, Necessary Assumption**, and **Principle** skew way more towards Internal Logic.\\\\n\\\\n**Flaw, Strengthen, Weaken**, and **Evaluate** are more likely to test our ability to think of potential objections like we\'re engaged in Courtroom Debate.\\",\\r\\n4760:      \\"left_md\\": \\"**Analogies** are two _specific_ cases that share some _general_ trait or relationship.\\\\n\\\\n# TRY THIS:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\"Kittens are to cats, what puppies are to _____.\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\nYou know the answer is _dogs_ because puppies and dogs have the same general relationship that kittens and cats have (youth vs. adult). They are analogous on at least one level.\\\\n\\\\nBecause we, as law students, will be tasked with reading judges\' opinions and digesting their full meaning, LSAC wants to test us on how well we pick up on implied ideas. We most commonly talk about implied ideas with the word Assumption (unstated, but implied ideas in an argument). But we can also talk about implied ideas in the sense of Inference (Must Be True / Most Supported), because inferences are _implications / consequences / conclusions_ that can be drawn from the information. And we can talk about implied ideas when it comes to **Completing Analogies**.\\\\n\\\\nA lot of Fill in the Blank questions are testing our ability to Complete the Analogy. The structural signpost of an analogy instructs the reader to **carry through** a trait or relationship that was described in the 1st case over to the 2nd case.\\",\\r\\n4782:    \\"slug\\": \\"w3-12-conditional-logic-easier-set-2\\",\\r\\n4790:      \\"Must Be True\\",\\r\\n4815:      \\"left_md\\": \\"Focus on finding **Conditional Indicator** words and using them as a way to organize how you write the **If / Then** rule.\\\\n\\\\nWrite or think through the contrapositive as well. Beware the temptation to make illegal Backwards or Opposite moves.\\\\n\\\\nThe LR question types that most often would prompt us to consider diagramming the Conditional Logic are\\\\n\\\\n- Must Be True\\\\n- Most Supported\\\\n- Assumption (mostly Sufficient, but some Necessary)\\\\n- Parallel / Parallel Flaw\\\\n- Principle-Strengthen\\\\n- Principle-Conform\\",\\r\\n4816:      \\"right_md\\": \\"Try these 7 problems featuring **conditional logic**  \\\\nlevels 2 and 3\\\\n\\\\nTry them all within 12 minutes, then circle back and confirm your thinking (untimed) before checking the answer / explanation.\\\\n\\\\n- **Principle-Strengthen w/ Principle in Stimulus** (diagram these conditionals, because each answer is testing us on whether we have an argument that establishes the left side of a rule and concludes the right side of a rule)\\\\n\\\\n- **Parallel** (usually diagram these arguments in the abstract, using generic variables like A, B, C. The more specific we are with seeing the abstract recipe, the more picky and decisive we can be with answers.)\\\\n\\\\n- **Assumption** (the conclusion gives us an A --> Z logic path we want to prove, and the evidence + the correct answer choice assemble together to create that A --> Z path)\\\\n\\\\n- **Must Be True / Most Supported** (if there are multiple conditionals, they will often chain together. otherwise, a fact provided will trigger the contrapositive of a conditional)\\\\n\\\\n- **Flaw / Parallel Flaw** (Flaw + Conditional Logic usually means the **Necessary vs. Sufficient** flaw, which is using a rule in an illegal Backwards or Opposite fashion. Sometimes it involves messing up Or / And stuff)\\\\n\\\\n---\\"\\r\\n4821:        \\"Must Be True\\",\\r\\n5006:      \\"left_md\\": \\"All question types in the Assumption Family will sometimes test a mathematical **conditional** **logic** understanding and other times test more \\\\\\"shades of gray\\\\\\" thinking like **comparisons** and **causal** explanations. But different question types do have strong tendencies. The graphic below shows an approximate continuum. We expect more formal logical thinking towards the top and more fuzzy real-world conversation towards the bottom.\\\\n**Internal Logic**\\\\nmissing links\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Sufficient Assumption**\\\\nmissing facts\\\\nrules, definitions\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Principle**\\\\nand vs. or\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0(strengthen / conform)\\\\nconditional logic\\\\ntrigger = evidence\\\\noutcome = conclusion\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Necessary Assumption**\\\\nruling out all alternatives\\\\n**Famous Flaws** **Flaw / Parallel Flaw**\\\\n**Courtroom Debate**\\\\nadvantages, disadvantages\\\\nrebuttals\\\\nrecommendations\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Strengthen**\\\\npredictions\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Weaken**\\\\nhypotheses\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0**Evaluate**\\\\nsurveys\\\\nexperiments\\\\nstatistics\\",\\r\\n5007:      \\"right_md\\": \\"## **Try these 10 problems, untimed.**\\\\n\\\\nThink about shifting your thinking back and forth between different reasoning structures\\\\n\\\\n**conditional** thinking\\\\n- rules, definitions, trigger, outcome, contrapositive\\\\n\\\\n**causal** thinking\\\\n- curious fact / author\'s explanation\\\\n- alternate explanations (reverse cause / 3rd factor)\\\\n- plausibility of author\'s explanation (no cause, no effect / effect without cause / cause without effect)\\\\n\\\\n**comparison** thinking\\\\n- pros vs. cons\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0/\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0weighing tradeoffs\\\\n- current world vs.\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0Plan/Prediction world\\\\n- then vs. now\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0/\\\\u00a0\\\\u00a0now vs. the future\\\\n- this group vs. that group\\\\n- sample vs. general population\\\\n- part vs. whole\\\\n- that situation vs. this situation\\\\n\\\\nAnd when you\'re doing **Flaw** , also consider the 10 **Famous Flaws.**\\"\\r\\n5170:      \\"right_md\\": \\"Try to predict what\'s missing. Sometimes, your intuition will clearly hear something missing. Other times, you\'ll need to ask yourself questions in order to find it.\\\\n\\\\n- Which claim is the Conclusion?\\\\n- Is there any wording appearing in the conclusion that never shows up in the evidence?\\\\n- If so, what concept in the evidence is that _New Wording in the Conclusion_ supposed to match up with?\\\\n- Are there any conditional rules or principles provided? If so has the author clearly established that the trigger is true?\\\\n\\\\nAs you review these problems,\\\\n**correct answers -**\\\\ndoes it supply a Missing Link? Was it the contrapositive of your prediction? did it address a Missing Fact that we need to trigger a conditional rule or principle?\\\\n\\\\n**incorrect answers -**\\\\ndoes it have the **Wrong Strength** ?\\\\ndoes it lack some **Needed Idea** (such as a New Idea in the conclusion)\\\\nis it **Irrelevant** for some reason?\\\\nit is an illegal **Reversal / Negation** of the connection we wanted?\\"\\r\\n"}'}]

\\"left_md\\": \\"Focus on finding **Conditional Indicator** words and using them as a way to organize how you write the **If / Then** rule.\\\\n\\\\nWrite or think through the contrapositive as well. Beware the temptation to make illegal Backwards or Opposite moves.\\\\n\\\\nThe LR question types that most often would prompt us to consider diagramming the Conditional Logic are\\\\n\\\\n- Must Be True\\\\n- Most Supported\\\\n- Assumption (mostly Sufficient than Necessary)\\\\n- Parallel / Parallel Flaw\\\\n- Principle-Strengthen / Principle-Conform\\",\\n      \\"right_md\\": \\"# Try these 9 problems, level 1-2\\\\n\\\\nTry them all within 12 minutes, then circle back and confirm your thinking (untimed) before confirming your answer and reading the explanation, if available.\\\\n\\\\nHow does Conditional Logic show up, by question type?\\\\n\\\\n- **Principle** (the left side of the Principle matches the Evidence, the right side matches the Conclusion)\\\\n\\\\n- **Parallel** (diagramming these arguments with \\\\\\"algebraic recipes\\\\\\" allows us to be more picky and decisive when evaluating answers)\\\\n\\\\n- **Assumption** (the conclusion gives us an A --> Z logic path we want to prove, and the evidence + the correct answer choice assemble together to create that A --> Z path / or the evidence gives us a conditional rule that is being used in drawing the conclusion, but we still need an answer choice to tell us something in order to clearly trigger that rule.)\\\\n\\\\n- **Must Be True / Most Supported** (multiple conditionals often chain together, and provided facts often match the contrapositive-trigger of a conditional and allow us to infer the contrapositive-outcome)\\\\n\\\\n- **Flaw / Parallel Flaw** (Flaw + Conditional Logic usually means the **Necessary vs. Sufficient** flaw, which is using a rule in an illegal Backwards or Opposite fashion. First Taste\\",\\n    \\"slug\\": \\"w2-03-must-be-true-first-taste\\",\\n    \\"category\\": \\"Process\\",\\n    \\"estimated_minutes\\": 50,\\n    \\"question_count\\": 6,\\n    \\"difficulty_min\\": 1,\\n    \\"difficulty_max\\": 5,\\n    \\"question_types\\": [\\n      \\"Must Be True\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"question_features\\": [],\\n    \\"reasoning_types\\": [\\n      \\"Conditional Logic\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"start_button_label\\": \\"Continue\\",\\n    \\"instruction_card\\": {\\n      \\"left_md\\": \\"# Must Be True recap\\\\n\\\\n## keywords\\\\n\\\\nstatements, information, passage, must be true, properly inferred, follows logically\\\\n\\\\n## examples\\\\n\\\\nIf the statements are true, which one of the following must be true?  \\\\nWhich of the following can be inferred by the information in the passage?\\\\n\\\\n## stimulus tendencies\\\\n\\\\nThese are FACTS, not ARGUMENTS.  \\\\nThe stimulus usually features some blend of Conditional or Quantified ideas.\\\\n\\\\n## correct answer\\\\n\\\\nIt is provable based on only the provided information. It does not need to use all the facts; in fact, it\'s a very common template that the inference involves combining the first and last claims (while the middle claim acts as filler). It\'s very rare for the correct answer to be derived from only one idea, so you should normally feel like in order to support the correct answer, you need to combine 2 or more facts.\\\\n\\\\nSince we\'re trying to pick an answer we can prove, we\'d prefer seeing weaker language. It\'s easier to prove a weakly worded claim. The more specific we are with seeing the abstract recipe, the more picky and decisive we can be with answers.)\\\\n\\\\n- **Assumption** (the conclusion gives us an A --> Z logic path we want to prove, and the evidence + the correct answer choice assemble together to create that A --> Z path)\\\\n\\\\n- **Must Be True / Most Supported** (if there are multiple conditionals, they will often chain together. otherwise, a fact provided will trigger the contrapositive of a conditional)\\\\n\\\\n- **Flaw / Parallel Flaw** (Flaw + Conditional Logic usually means the **Necessary vs. Sufficient** flaw, which is using a rule in an illegal Backwards or Opposite fashion. Sometimes it involves messing up Or / And stuff)\\\\n\\\\n---\\"\\n    },\\n    \\"bank_filter\\": {\\n      \\"qtypes\\": [\\n        \\"Most Strongly Supported\\",\\n        \\"Must Be True\\",\\n        \\"Sufficient Assumption\\",\\n        \\"Parallel Reasoning\\",\\n        \\"Parallel\\",\\n        \\"Parallel method of reasoning\\",\\n        \\"Parallel Method of Reasoning\\",\\n        \\"Parallel method of reasoning (Parallel)\\",\\n        \\"Parallel Reasoning: Complete Argument\\",\\n        \\"Parallel Reasoning: Questionable\\",\\n        \\"Parallel Flaw\\",\\n        \\"Parallel flawed method of reasoning\\",\\n        \\"Flawed Parallel Reasoning\\",\\n        \\"Parallel flawed method of reasoning (Parallel, Flaw)\\",\\n        \\"Principle-Strengthen\\",\\n        \\"Principle-Conform\\",\\n        \\"Flaw\\"\\n      ],\\n      \\"features\\": [\\n        \\"Conditional Diagramming\\"\\n      ],\\n      \\"reasoning_types\\": [],\\n      \\"difficulty_min\\": 2,\\n      \\"difficulty_max\\": 3,\\n      \\"count\\": 7\\n    },\\n    \\"week\\": 3,\\n    \\"order_in_week\\": 12,\\n    \\"source_file\\": \\"week_3_blue_lr_guided_drills.md\\",\\n    \\"level\\": \\"beginner\\"\\n  }\\n]\\n"}'}]

Every assign surface in the\\r\\n * workspace narrows to this union, so there is exactly one place that knows\\r\\n * how work reaches a student.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type AssignableItem =\\r\\n  | { kind: \'homework_set\'; setId: string }\\r\\n  | { kind: \'guided_drill\'; drillId: string }\\r\\n  | { kind: \'activity\'; activityId: string }\\r\\n  | { kind: \'bootcamp\'; topicId: string };\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface StudentResult {\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * The assignment row. Absent for bootcamps, which have no row of their own.\\r\\n   * On a bundle this is the first item\'s row; see `assignmentIds` for all of them.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  assignmentId?: string;\\r\\n  classroomItemId?: string;\\r\\n  route?: string;\\r\\n  /** Every assignment row written for this student, in bundle order. */\\r\\n  assignmentIds?: string[];\\r\\n  /** Every classroom item created for this student, in bundle order. */\\r\\n  classroomItemIds?: string[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Per-channel notify outcome. A false channel means the work landed and the\\r\\n   * student can see it, but one message did not go out.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  notify?: NotifyResult;\\r\\n  /** Set only on `failed`: why this student got nothing. */\\r\\n  error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface AssignWorkResult {\\r\\n  ok: StudentResult[];\\r\\n  failed: StudentResult[];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** What one kind of item needs, resolved once before the per-student loop. */\\r\\ninterface AssignPlan {\\r\\n  type: ClassroomItemType;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  description?: string;\\r\\n  /** Wording for the notification (\\"New guided drill\\", etc). */\\r\\n  assignmentType?: string;\\r\\n  sourceTable: string;\\r\\n  /** Insert the assignment row. Returns null for kinds that have no row. */\\r\\n  insertRow: (studentId: string) => Promise<string | null>;\\r\\n  sourceId: (assignmentId: string | null) => string | undefined;\\r\\n  route: (assignmentId: string | null) => string;\\r\\n  unlockAt: string | null;\\r\\n  dueAt: string | null;\\r\\n  metadata: Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction message(error: unknown, fallback: string) {\\r\\n  return error instanceof Error ? error.message : fallback;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Per-student proficiency, resolved once for the whole assign. */\\r\\nexport type StudentLevelMap = ReadonlyMap<string, StudentLevel | null>;\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Postgres 42703 (undefined_column), which PostgREST also surfaces in the\\r\\n * message. Means the migration has not been applied to this database yet.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction isMissingColumnError(error: unknown): boolean {\\r\\n  const e = error as { code?: string; message?: string } | null;\\r\\n  if (!e) return false;\\r\\n  if (e.code === \'42703\') return true;\\r\\n  return /proficiency_level/.test(e.message ?? \'\') && /does not exist|column/i.test(e.message ?? \'\');\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Every student\'s band in one query.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Seeded with null for each requested id, so a student with no profile row is a\\r\\n * defined \\"no level\\" rather than an undefined lookup. A student Joshua has not\\r\\n * levelled gets null and therefore the drill\'s own range -- the behaviour that\\r\\n * predates levels. Inventing a default here would be the system overruling him.\\r\\n */\\r\\nasync function fetchStudentLevels(studentIds: string[]): Promise<StudentLevelMap> {\\r\\n  const levels = new Map<string, StudentLevel | null>(\\r\\n    studentIds.map((id) => [id, null]),\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  if (studentIds.length === 0) return levels;\\r\\n\\r\\n  // proficiency_level is newer than the auto-generated Supabase types, which\\r\\n  // are not hand-editable, so the select is cast at the call site -- the same\\r\\n  // pattern savedSets.ts and questionExposure.ts use for new columns.\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await (supabase.from(\'profiles\') as never as {\\r\\n    select: (cols: string) => {\\r\\n      in: (\\r\\n        col: string,\\r\\n        vals: string[],\\r\\n      ) => Promise<{ data: Array<Record<string, unknown>> | null; error: unknown }>;\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  })\\r\\n    .select(\'class_id, proficiency_level\')\\r\\n    .in(\'class_id\', studentIds);\\r\\n\\r\\n  // The code and the migration do not land atomically. If the app is live\\r\\n  // before the column exists, treat it as \\"nobody has a level yet\\" and assign\\r\\n  // exactly as we did before levels existed, rather than breaking every guided\\r\\n  // drill assign until the migration catches up. Any other error is real and\\r\\n  // fails the assign cleanly, because silently unlevelling work Joshua asked to\\r\\n  // be levelled would be worse than not assigning it.\\r\\n  if (error) {\\r\\n    if (isMissingColumnError(error)) {\\r\\n      console.warn(\'[assignWork] profiles.proficiency_level is not deployed yet; assigning unlevelled\');\\r\\n      return levels;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    throw error;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  for (const row of data ?? []) {\\r\\n    const id = row.class_id as string;\\r\\n    if (!levels.has(id)) continue;\\r\\n    levels.set(id, coerceStudentLevel(row.proficiency_level, null));\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return levels;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function planFor(\\r\\n  item: AssignableItem,\\r\\n  teacherId: string,\\r\\n  scheduledForLocalDate: string | undefined,\\r\\n  levels: StudentLevelMap,\\r\\n  levelOverrides: Record<string, StudentLevel | null> | undefined,\\r\\n  questionCountOverride: number | null | undefined,\\r\\n  frozenQidsByStudent: Record<string, string[]> | undefined,\\r\\n): Promise<AssignPlan> {\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * `studentId in overrides` rather than `??`, so an explicit null (\\"assign\\r\\n   * this one unlevelled\\") is honoured instead of falling through to the profile.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  const levelFor = (studentId: string): StudentLevel | null =>\\r\\n    levelOverrides && studentId in levelOverrides\\r\\n      ? levelOverrides[studentId]\\r\\n      : (levels.get(studentId) ?? null);\\r\\n\\r\\n  // A scheduled date is a calendar day the teacher picked, so it has to be read\\r\\n  // in their timezone. Parsed as UTC it would unlock the evening before.\\r\\n  const scheduledDay = scheduledForLocalDate ? parseLocalDay(scheduledForLocalDate) : null;\\r\\n  if (scheduledForLocalDate && !scheduledDay) {\\r\\n    throw new Error(`Not a calendar date: ${scheduledForLocalDate}`);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const unlockAt = scheduledDay ? scheduledDay.toISOString() : null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  switch (item.kind) {\\r\\n    case \'homework_set\': {\\r\\n      const { data: set, error } = await supabase\\r\\n        .from(\'homework_sets\')\\r\\n        .select(\'id, title, description, question_qids\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'id\', item.setId)\\r\\n        .single();\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n\\r\\n      return {\\r\\n        type: \'homework\',\\r\\n        title: set.title,\\r\\n        description: set.description ?? undefined,\\r\\n        assignmentType: \'homework\',\\r\\n        sourceTable: \'homework_assignments\',\\r\\n        insertRow: async (studentId) => {\\r\\n          const { data, error: insertError } = await supabase\\r\\n            .from(\'homework_assignments\')\\r\\n            .insert({\\r\\n              set_id: set.id,\\r\\n              student_id: studentId,\\r\\n              // The student owns the row, not the set\'s author. Pointing this"}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"f98325","wall_time_seconds":10.0002397,"session_id":41279,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}}'}]

That is the whole point of the pool model.\\r\\n          //\\r\\n          // Freezing is correct in exactly one case — an offline artifact was\\r\\n          // generated from a specific list and emailed. Then the set has\\r\\n          // already left the building, and a drill that re-draws would serve\\r\\n          // different questions from the file in the student\'s inbox. Pinning\\r\\n          // here is what makes the two provably the same.\\r\\n          //\\r\\n          // The cost is real and deliberate: a pinned assignment stops\\r\\n          // re-drawing, and resolveLeveledDrillQids returns a pin in full,\\r\\n          // unfiltered by band, so a later level edit on this row is inert.\\r\\n          const frozen = frozenQidsByStudent?.[studentId];\\r\\n          const { data, error: insertError } = await supabase\\r\\n            .from(\'guided_drill_assignments\')\\r\\n            .insert({\\r\\n              guided_drill_id: drill.id,\\r\\n              student_id: studentId,\\r\\n              class_id: studentId,\\r\\n              assigned_by: teacherId,\\r\\n              scheduled_for: scheduledForLocalDate ?? null,\\r\\n              unlock_at: unlockAt,\\r\\n              status: \'assigned\',\\r\\n              level: levelFor(studentId),\\r\\n              question_count: questionCountOverride ?? null,\\r\\n              // Spread rather than `question_qids: frozen ?? null`, so the\\r\\n              // default path sends a payload with no such key at all. That is\\r\\n              // what makes \\"unchanged when not freezing\\" checkable in a spec\\r\\n              // rather than merely asserted here.\\r\\n              ...(frozen && frozen.length > 0 ? { question_qids: frozen } : {}),\\r\\n            } as never)\\r\\n            .select(\'id\')\\r\\n            .single();\\r\\n          if (insertError) throw insertError;\\r\\n          return data.id as string;\\r\\n        },\\r\\n        sourceId: (assignmentId) => assignmentId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        route: (assignmentId) =>\\r\\n          assignmentId\\r\\n            ? `/guided/${assignmentId}?returnTo=${encodeURIComponent(\'/classroom\')}`\\r\\n            : \'/classroom\',\\r\\n        unlockAt,\\r\\n        dueAt: null,\\r\\n        metadata: {\\r\\n          guided_drill_id: drill.id,\\r\\n          // The only durable record of WHY this one row is pinned. Six weeks\\r\\n          // later, \\"why did this drill not re-draw?\\" has an answer.\\r\\n          ...(frozenQidsByStudent ? { frozen_reason: \'artifact_attached\' } : {}),\\r\\n        },\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    case \'activity\': {\\r\\n      const { data: activity, error } = await supabase\\r\\n        .from(\'activities\')\\r\\n        .select(\'*\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'id\', item.activityId)\\r\\n        .single();\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n\\r\\n      return {\\r\\n        type: \'activity\',\\r\\n        title: activity.title,\\r\\n        description: activity.subtitle ?? undefined,\\r\\n        assignmentType: \'activity\',\\r\\n        sourceTable: \'activity_assignments\',\\r\\n        insertRow: async (studentId) => {\\r\\n          // Content is snapshotted at assign time so editing the activity\\r\\n          // later cannot change work a student has already started.\\r\\n          const { data, error: insertError } = await supabase\\r\\n            .from(\'activity_assignments\')\\r\\n            .insert({\\r\\n              activity_id: activity.id,\\r\\n              student_id: studentId,\\r\\n              class_id: studentId,\\r\\n              assigned_by: teacherId,\\r\\n              title: activity.title,\\r\\n              subtitle: activity.subtitle,\\r\\n              instructions: activity.instructions as never,\\r\\n              item_format: activity.item_format,\\r\\n              items: activity.items as never,\\r\\n              // Snapshotted for the same reason `items` is. The clock is what\\r\\n              // her time is scored against, so a later edit to the Activity\\r\\n              // must not be able to rescore a run she has already finished.\\r\\n              mode: activity.mode,\\r\\n              time_goal_minutes: activity.time_goal_minutes as never,\\r\\n              source_section: activity.source_section,\\r\\n              status: \'assigned\',\\r\\n            })\\r\\n            .select(\'id\')\\r\\n            .single();\\r\\n          if (insertError) throw insertError;\\r\\n          return data.id as string;\\r\\n        },\\r\\n        sourceId: (assignmentId) => assignmentId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        route: (assignmentId) => (assignmentId ? `/activities/${assignmentId}` : \'/activities\'),\\r\\n        unlockAt,\\r\\n        dueAt: null,\\r\\n        metadata: { activity_id: activity.id },\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    case \'bootcamp\': {\\r\\n      const bootcamp = BOOTCAMPS.find((b) => b.id === item.topicId);\\r\\n      if (!bootcamp) throw new Error(`Unknown bootcamp: ${item.topicId}`);\\r\\n\\r\\n      return {\\r\\n        type: \'bootcamp_module\',\\r\\n        title: bootcamp.title,\\r\\n        description: bootcamp.description,\\r\\n        assignmentType: \'bootcamp\',\\r\\n        // A bootcamp has no per-student row: the classroom item IS the\\r\\n        // assignment, and source_id is the bootcamp\'s own route.\\r\\n        sourceTable: \'bootcamps\',\\r\\n        insertRow: async () => null,\\r\\n        sourceId: () => bootcamp.route,\\r\\n        route: () => bootcamp.route,\\r\\n        unlockAt,\\r\\n        dueAt: null,\\r\\n        metadata: { bootcamp_id: bootcamp.id },\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Assign one piece of work to one or more students.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Per student, in sequence: insert the assignment row, create the classroom\\r\\n * item that makes it visible, then notify. Students are isolated from each\\r\\n * other, so one failure never costs the rest their assignment and never\\r\\n * re-inserts a row that already landed.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * If the classroom item or the notify call throws, the rows already written\\r\\n * for that student are deleted again: an assignment the student cannot see is\\r\\n * worse than no assignment, because it silently blocks a re-assign. A notify\\r\\n * call that returns with a channel `false` is NOT a failure — the work is\\r\\n * visible in the Classroom and only a message went missing, so it is reported\\r\\n * on the ok result rather than rolled back.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function assignWork(input: {\\r\\n  teacherId: string;\\r\\n  studentIds: string[];\\r\\n  item: AssignableItem;\\r\\n  /** A local calendar day, YYYY-MM-DD. Unlocks the work that morning. */\\r\\n  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Teacher-authored overrides for what the student sees. The assign builder\\r\\n   * lets Joshua retitle a set or set a due date for one student without\\r\\n   * editing the set itself.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  overrides?: {\\r\\n    title?: string;\\r\\n    description?: string;\\r\\n    dueAt?: string | null;\\r\\n    metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Per-student band, overriding their profile level. An explicit null assigns\\r\\n   * that student unlevelled. Guided drills only.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  levelOverrides?: Record<string, StudentLevel | null>;\\r\\n  /** Shorten the drill for everyone in this assign. Guided drills only. */\\r\\n  questionCountOverride?: number | null;\\r\\n  /** See assignWorkBundle. Guided drills only. */\\r\\n  frozenQidsByStudent?: Record<string, string[]>;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Send the student a notification. Default true. Set false only when the\\r\\n   * caller sends its own message covering this work.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  notify?: boolean;\\r\\n}): Promise<AssignWorkResult> {\\r\\n  return runAssign({ ...input, items: [input.item] });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Assign several pieces of work — of mixed kinds — as ONE event (spec D4).\\r\\n *\\r\\n * A drill and a homework set for the same topic ship together: the student gets\\r\\n * a classroom card for each (they are separate work), but a single notification,\\r\\n * because two pings for one handoff reads as two assignments. Per student the\\r\\n * bundle is all-or-nothing: if the third item fails, the first two are rolled\\r\\n * back, so the teacher never has to reason about a half-delivered bundle.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Per-item overrides are deliberately not offered here — retitling one item\\r\\n * inside a bundle belongs to the single-item path.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function assignWorkBundle(input: {\\r\\n  teacherId: string;\\r\\n  studentIds: string[];\\r\\n  items: AssignableItem[];\\r\\n  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;\\r\\n  /** Per-student band. Applies to every guided drill in the bundle. */\\r\\n  levelOverrides?: Record<string, StudentLevel | null>;\\r\\n  questionCountOverride?: number | null;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Pin an exact question list on the guided-drill row instead of drawing at\\r\\n   * open time. Keyed by studentId, because two students at different bands\\r\\n   * freeze different sets from the same drill.\\r\\n   *\\r\\n   * Set this ONLY when an offline artifact is produced from the same list.\\r\\n   * A pinned assignment stops re-drawing on re-attempt, and a later level\\r\\n   * change on that row has no effect. Guided drills only.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  frozenQidsByStudent?: Record<string, string[]>;\\r\\n  /** See assignWork. Default true. */\\r\\n  notify?: boolean;\\r\\n}): Promise<AssignWorkResult> {\\r\\n  return runAssign(input);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** What one student ended up with, so a failure can be undone precisely. */\\r\\ninterface Written {\\r\\n  assignmentId: string | null;\\r\\n  classroomItemId: string | null;\\r\\n  table: string;\\r\\n  kind: AssignableItem[\'kind\'];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function runAssign(input: {\\r\\n  teacherId: string;\\r\\n  studentIds: string[];\\r\\n  items: AssignableItem[];\\r\\n  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;\\r\\n  overrides?: {\\r\\n    title?: string;\\r\\n    description?: string;\\r\\n    dueAt?: string | null;\\r\\n    metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  levelOverrides?: Record<string, StudentLevel | null>;\\r\\n  questionCountOverride?: number | null;\\r\\n  frozenQidsByStudent?: Record<string, string[]>;\\r\\n  notify?: boolean;\\r\\n}): Promise<AssignWorkResult> {\\r\\n  const {\\r\\n    teacherId,\\r\\n    studentIds,\\r\\n    items,\\r\\n    scheduledForLocalDate,\\r\\n    overrides,\\r\\n    levelOverrides,\\r\\n    questionCountOverride,\\r\\n    frozenQidsByStudent,\\r\\n    notify: shouldNotify = true,\\r\\n  } = input;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const ok: StudentResult[] = [];\\r\\n  const failed: StudentResult[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (studentIds.length === 0 || items.length === 0) return { ok, failed };\\r\\n\\r\\n  /*\\r\\n   * Freezing is validated before any write, and it throws rather than\\r\\n   * degrading. A silent no-op here is the exact failure this feature exists to\\r\\n   * prevent: an artifact would be emailed carrying questions the assignment\\r\\n   * row does not hold, and nothing downstream could tell.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  if (frozenQidsByStudent) {\\r\\n    if (!items.some((item) => item.kind === \'guided_drill\')) {\\r\\n      throw new Error(\'frozenQidsByStudent was supplied but no guided drill is being assigned\');\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    for (const [studentId, qids] of Object.entries(frozenQidsByStudent)) {\\r\\n      if (!studentIds.includes(studentId)) {\\r\\n        throw new Error(`frozenQidsByStudent names ${studentId}, who is not in this assign`);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      if (!Array.isArray(qids) || qids.length === 0) {\\r\\n        throw new Error(`frozenQidsByStudent[${studentId}] is empty; omit the student instead`);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Every item is resolved before anyone is written to, so an unreadable item\\r\\n  // fails the whole assign cleanly instead of half-delivering the bundle. An\\r\\n  // unreadable student level belongs in the same block for the same reason: the\\r\\n  // catch below reports every student as failed and nothing has been written.\\r\\n  let plans: AssignPlan[];\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    // Only guided drills carry a level, so nothing else pays for the lookup.\\r\\n    const needsLevels = items.some((item) => item.kind === \'guided_drill\');\\r\\n    const levels: StudentLevelMap = needsLevels\\r\\n      ? await fetchStudentLevels(studentIds)\\r\\n      : new Map();\\r\\n\\r\\n    plans = await Promise.all(\\r\\n      items.map(async (item, index) => {\\r\\n        const base = await planFor(\\r\\n          item,\\r\\n          teacherId,\\r\\n          scheduledForLocalDate,\\r\\n          levels,\\r\\n          levelOverrides,\\r\\n          questionCountOverride,\\r\\n          frozenQidsByStudent,\\r\\n        );\\r\\n        // Overrides only ever accompany a single item (see assignWork).\\r\\n        if (index > 0 || !overrides) return base;\\r\\n        return {\\r\\n          ...base,\\r\\n          title: overrides.title?.trim() || base.title,\\r\\n          description: overrides.description?.trim() || base.description,\\r\\n          dueAt: overrides.dueAt !== undefined ? overrides.dueAt : base.dueAt,\\r\\n          metadata: { ...base.metadata, ...(overrides.metadata ?? {}) },\\r\\n        };\\r\\n      }),\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  } catch (error) {\\r\\n    // Nothing was written, so every student failed for the same reason.\\r\\n    const reason = message(error, \'Could not load the item to assign\');\\r\\n    return { ok, failed: studentIds.map((studentId) => ({ studentId, error: reason })) };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  for (const studentId of studentIds) {\\r\\n    const written: Written[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const routes: string[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n      for (let i = 0; i < plans.length; i++) {\\r\\n        const plan = plans[i];\\r\\n        const record: Written = {\\r\\n          assignmentId: null,\\r\\n          classroomItemId: null,\\r\\n          table: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          kind: items[i].kind,\\r\\n        };\\r\\n        // Recorded before each write so a throw mid-item still rolls back what\\r\\n        // the previous items wrote.\\r\\n        written.push(record);\\r\\n\\r\\n        record.assignmentId = await plan.insertRow(studentId);\\r\\n        const route = plan.route(record.assignmentId);\\r\\n        routes.push(route);\\r\\n\\r\\n        const classroomItem = await createClassroomItem({\\r\\n          studentId,\\r\\n          createdBy: teacherId,\\r\\n          type: plan.type,\\r\\n          title: plan.title,\\r\\n          description: plan.description,\\r\\n          sourceTable: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          sourceId: plan.sourceId(record.assignmentId),\\r\\n          unlockAt: plan.unlockAt,\\r\\n          dueAt: plan.dueAt,\\r\\n          visibleToStudent: true,\\r\\n          status: \'assigned\',\\r\\n          metadata: { ...plan.metadata, assigned_route: route },\\r\\n        });\\r\\n        record.classroomItemId = classroomItem.id;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n\\r\\n      // A caller that owns its own messaging opts out. The weekly-plan builder\\r\\n      // does: it sends one summary for the whole week, and seven bell entries\\r\\n      // for one plan is the exact UX it already avoids.\\r\\n      const summary = notificationFor(plans, routes);\\r\\n      const notify = shouldNotify\\r\\n        ? await notifyStudentOfClassroomItem({\\r\\n            adminUserId: teacherId,\\r\\n            studentId,\\r\\n            title: summary.title,\\r\\n            description: summary.description,\\r\\n            route: summary.route,\\r\\n            assignmentType: summary.assignmentType,\\r\\n          })\\r\\n        : undefined;\\r\\n\\r\\n      ok.push({\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        assignmentId: written[0]?.assignmentId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        classroomItemId: written[0]?.classroomItemId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        route: routes[0],\\r\\n        assignmentIds: written.map((w) => w.assignmentId).filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        classroomItemIds: written\\r\\n          .map((w) => w.classroomItemId)\\r\\n          .filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        notify,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    } catch (error) {\\r\\n      console.error(\'[assignWork] student failed\', {\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        kinds: items.map((i) => i.kind),\\r\\n        written,\\r\\n        error,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      // Newest first, so the half-written item goes before the ones that landed.\\r\\n      for (const record of [...written].reverse()) {\\r\\n        await rollback(record);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      failed.push({ studentId, error: message(error, \'Assign failed\') });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { ok, failed };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One handoff, one message. A single item keeps its own wording; a bundle is\\r\\n * announced as a list pointing at the Classroom, since no one route covers it.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction notificationFor(\\r\\n  plans: AssignPlan[],\\r\\n  routes: string[],\\r\\n): { title: string; description?: string; route: string; assignmentType?: string } {\\r\\n  if (plans.length === 1) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      title: plans[0].title,\\r\\n      description: plans[0].description,\\r\\n      route: routes[0],\\r\\n      assignmentType: plans[0].assignmentType,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    title: `${plans.length} new pieces of work`,\\r\\n    description: plans.map((p) => `• ${p.title}`).join(\'\\\\n\'),\\r\\n    route: \'/classroom\',\\r\\n    assignmentType: \'assignment\',\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One sentence for the teacher, in the order they care about: who missed out,\\r\\n * then which messages did not go, then plain success. Shared so every assign\\r\\n * surface reports the same outcome the same way.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function describeAssignResult(\\r\\n  result: AssignWorkResult,\\r\\n  title: string,\\r\\n): { level: \'success\' | \'warning\' | \'error\'; message: string } {\\r\\n  const { ok, failed } = result;\\r\\n  const students = (n: number) => `${n} student${n === 1 ? \'\' : \'s\'}`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (ok.length === 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'error\',\\r\\n      message: failed[0]?.error\\r\\n        ? `Could not assign \\"${title}\\": ${failed[0].error}`\\r\\n        : `Could not assign \\"${title}\\"`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (failed.length > 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'warning\',\\r\\n      message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}. ${students(failed.length)} failed: ${failed[0].error ?? \'unknown error\'}`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const quiet = ok.filter((r) => r.notify && !(r.notify.inbox_ok && r.notify.bell_ok && r.notify.email_ok));\\r\\n  if (quiet.length > 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'warning\',\\r\\n      message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}, but ${students(quiet.length)} were not fully notified.`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { level: \'success\', message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}` };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Undo a half-written assign. Best effort: if the cleanup itself fails there is\\r\\n * nothing further to try, so it is logged loudly and swallowed rather than\\r\\n * masking the original error that sent us here.\\r\\n */\\r\\nasync function rollback(input: {\\r\\n  assignmentId: string | null;\\r\\n  classroomItemId: string | null;\\r\\n  table: string;\\r\\n  kind: AssignableItem[\'kind\'];\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const { assignmentId, classroomItemId, table, kind } = input;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (classroomItemId) {\\r\\n    const { error } = await supabase.from(\'classroom_items\').delete().eq(\'id\', classroomItemId);\\r\\n    if (error) console.error(\'[assignWork] rollback left a classroom item\', { classroomItemId, error });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Bootcamps write no assignment row, so there is nothing else to undo.\\r\\n  if (!assignmentId || kind === \'bootcamp\') return;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { error } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(table as \'homework_assignments\' | \'guided_drill_assignments\' | \'activity_assignments\')\\r\\n    .delete()\\r\\n    .eq(\'id\', assignmentId);\\r\\n  if (error) console.error(\'[assignWork] rollback left an assignment row\', { table, assignmentId, error });\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 15.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c6c77b","wall_time_seconds":9.6313706,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":378,"output":"---\\r\\nname: computer-use\\r\\ndescription: Control Windows apps from ChatGPT\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Computer Use\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this skill to automate the UI of Microsoft Windows apps. It uses SendInput, UI Automation, and Windows.Graphics.Capture screenshots that work even when windows are occluded.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf this plugin is available, read this entire `SKILL.md` once before Windows automation work, before saying Computer Use is unavailable, and before falling back to other Windows automation.\\r\\n\\r\\nStart with the directions in the Initialize section below. Read these bundled Markdown files relative to this `SKILL.md` when you need information about the specific topic they cover:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `../../docs/guidance.md`: core runtime behavior, target-window workflow, screenshot handling, and recovery guidance. You MUST read this before controlling Windows apps.\\r\\n- `../../docs/api.md`: full `sky` API reference. Read this when you need method signatures or object shapes.\\r\\n- `../../docs/confirmations.md`: you MUST read this before deciding whether a Windows UI action needs confirmation\\r\\n\\r\\n## Initialize\\r\\n\\r\\nThe bundled `cua_node` `@oai/sky` package is the core entry point for Computer Use. Import it directly by package name from the JavaScript session. Do not spawn `codex-computer-use.exe`, search for the helper executable, or build a custom helper protocol client.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun this once per fresh `node_repl` JavaScript session:\\r\\n\\r\\n```js\\r\\nif (!globalThis.sky) {\\r\\n  const { sky } = await import(\\"@oai/sky\\");\\r\\n  globalThis.sky = sky;\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"29e9a6","wall_time_seconds":2.7375114,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":797,"output":"/**\\r\\n * The proficiency band a student works at, and how it maps onto the question\\r\\n * bank\'s 1-5 difficulty scale.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The bands overlap on purpose. Hard cutoffs (1-2 / 3 / 4-5) starve the middle\\r\\n * band: a drill narrowed to a single difficulty value runs out of questions on\\r\\n * any question type that is not one of the big four. Overlapping bands keep\\r\\n * every level drawing from a pool large enough to fill a full drill.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * This module deliberately has no imports. The bands are needed by the assign\\r\\n * path, the runtime resolver, the teacher UI and the tests, and anything that\\r\\n * imported the supabase client or the question bank singleton to reach them\\r\\n * would drag both into every one of those callers.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type StudentLevel = \'beginner\' | \'intermediate\' | \'advanced\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const STUDENT_LEVELS = [\'beginner\', \'intermediate\', \'advanced\'] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface DifficultyBand {\\r\\n  min: number;\\r\\n  max: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The one place the band numbers are defined in TypeScript. They appear in SQL\\r\\n * exactly once more, in the guided_drills.level backfill, and a spec asserts\\r\\n * that migration still agrees with this table.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport const LEVEL_BANDS: Record<StudentLevel, DifficultyBand> = {\\r\\n  beginner: { min: 1, max: 3 },\\r\\n  intermediate: { min: 2, max: 4 },\\r\\n  advanced: { min: 3, max: 5 },\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const LEVEL_LABELS: Record<StudentLevel, string> = {\\r\\n  beginner: \'Beginner\',\\r\\n  intermediate: \'Intermediate\',\\r\\n  advanced: \'Advanced\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The full difficulty range, used when no level applies. */\\r\\nexport const FULL_DIFFICULTY_RANGE: DifficultyBand = { min: 1, max: 5 };\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function isStudentLevel(value: unknown): value is StudentLevel {\\r\\n  return typeof value === \'string\' && (STUDENT_LEVELS as readonly string[]).includes(value);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Narrow an untrusted value (a DB column, a URL param) to a level.\\r\\n * Strict: \'Beginner\' and \'BEGINNER\' are not levels. The stored values are\\r\\n * lowercase by CHECK constraint, so anything else is a bug worth surfacing\\r\\n * rather than silently coercing.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function coerceStudentLevel<T>(value: unknown, fallback: T): StudentLevel | T {\\r\\n  return isStudentLevel(value) ? value : fallback;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function levelBand(level: StudentLevel): DifficultyBand {\\r\\n  return LEVEL_BANDS[level];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Midpoint of a band, used to rank out-of-band questions by nearness. */\\r\\nexport function bandMidpoint(band: DifficultyBand): number {\\r\\n  return (band.min + band.max) / 2;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function isInBand(difficulty: unknown, band: DifficultyBand): boolean {\\r\\n  // A non-finite difficulty is never in band. One question in the bank has a\\r\\n  // null difficulty, and the raw numeric comparisons used elsewhere let it pass\\r\\n  // every filter (null < 1 and null > 5 are both false).\\r\\n  if (typeof difficulty !== \'number\' || !Number.isFinite(difficulty)) return false;\\r\\n  return difficulty >= band.min && difficulty <= band.max;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Human-readable band, e.g. \\"difficulty 1-3\\". */\\r\\nexport function describeBand(band: DifficultyBand): string {\\r\\n  return band.min === band.max\\r\\n    ? `difficulty ${band.min}`\\r\\n    : `difficulty ${band.min}-${band.max}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"9038ef","wall_time_seconds":2.0609334,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":920,"output":"  console.log(`  ${flag} was off for ${who}. Turned it on, or they could not have opened this.`);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function main() {\\r\\n  const teacherId = await signIn();\\r\\n  const student = resolveStudent(await listStudents(), studentQuery!);\\r\\n  const who = `${student.display_name ?? \\"(no name)\\"} <${student.email ?? \\"?\\"}>`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (messageText) {\\r\\n    console.log(`Message → ${who}\\\\n  \\"${messageText}\\"`);\\r\\n    if (dryRun) return console.log(\\"\\\\n(dry run — nothing sent)\\");\\r\\n    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(teacherId, student.class_id);\\r\\n    const { error } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"messages\\")\\r\\n      .insert({ conversation_id: conversationId, sender_id: teacherId, body: messageText });\\r\\n    if (error) throw error;\\r\\n    return console.log(\\"Sent.\\");\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  let item: Parameters<typeof assignWork>[0][\\"item\\"];\\r\\n  let label: string;\\r\\n  let note = \\"\\";\\r\\n  if (activityQuery) {\\r\\n    const a = await resolveActivity(activityQuery);\\r\\n    item = { kind: \\"activity\\", activityId: a.id };\\r\\n    label = a.title;\\r\\n  } else if (drillQuery) {\\r\\n    const d = await resolveDrill(drillQuery);\\r\\n    item = { kind: \\"guided_drill\\", drillId: d.id };\\r\\n    label = d.title;\\r\\n  } else if (sectionQuery) {\\r\\n    const s = await findOrCreateSectionSet(teacherId, sectionQuery, !dryRun);\\r\\n    item = { kind: \\"homework_set\\", setId: s.id };\\r\\n    label = s.title;\\r\\n    note = `  ${s.count} questions, timed 35:00 · set ${s.created ? \\"created\\" : \\"reused\\"} (${s.id})`;\\r\\n  } else {\\r\\n    const s = await resolveSet(setQuery!);\\r\\n    item = { kind: \\"homework_set\\", setId: s.id };\\r\\n    label = s.title;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (item.kind === \\"homework_set\\") {\\r\\n    // The unique index refuses a second active copy; say so in plain words\\r\\n    // rather than surfacing a Postgres constraint name.\\r\\n    const { data: prior } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"homework_assignments\\")\\r\\n      .select(\\"id,status,created_at\\")\\r\\n      .eq(\\"student_id\\", student.class_id)\\r\\n      .eq(\\"set_id\\", item.setId)\\r\\n      .neq(\\"status\\", \\"completed\\")\\r\\n      .maybeSingle();\\r\\n    if (prior) {\\r\\n      console.error(\\r\\n        `assign: ${who} already has \\"${label}\\" (${prior.status}, assigned ${new Date(prior.created_at).toLocaleDateString(\\"en-CA\\")}).` +\\r\\n          `\\r\\n  /classroom/${prior.id}. A set can be re-issued only after that copy is completed.`,\\r\\n      );\\r\\n      process.exit(1);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  console.log(\\r\\n    `Assign \\"${label}\\" → ${who}` +\\r\\n      `${scheduleFor ? `  (unlocks ${scheduleFor})` : \\"\\"}${dueOn ? `  (due ${dueOn})` : \\"\\"}`,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  if (note) console.log(note);\\r\\n  if (dryRun) return console.log(\\"\\\\n(dry run — nothing written)\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { ok, failed } = await assignWork({\\r\\n    teacherId,\\r\\n    studentIds: [student.class_id],\\r\\n    item,\\r\\n    scheduledForLocalDate: scheduleFor,\\r\\n    overrides: dueOn ? { dueAt: dueAtEndOfDay(dueOn) } : undefined,\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (failed.length) {\\r\\n    console.error(`FAILED: ${failed[0].error}`);\\r\\n    process.exit(1);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const r = ok[0];\\r\\n  const n = r.notify;\\r\\n  // After the write, not before: a student who is never assigned anything has\\r\\n  // no business having a feature opened for them.\\r\\n  await ensureCanOpen(student.class_id, item.kind, who);\\r\\n  console.log(\\r\\n    `Assigned. id=${r.assignmentId}\\\\n` +\\r\\n      `  inbox ${n?.inbox_ok ? \\"ok\\" : \\"FAILED\\"} · bell ${n?.bell_ok ? \\"ok\\" : \\"FAILED\\"} · email ${n?.email_ok ? \\"ok\\" : \\"FAILED\\"}`,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  if (n && !(n.inbox_ok && n.bell_ok && n.email_ok)) {\\r\\n    console.warn(\\"! The work IS assigned and visible; only a notification channel failed.\\");\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nmain()\\r\\n  .then(() => process.exit(0))\\r\\n  .catch((e) => {\\r\\n    console.error(e instanceof Error ? e.message : e);\\r\\n    process.exit(1);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n"}'}]
