[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"28424e","wall_time_seconds":2.3863625,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1963,"output":"[\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT124-S3-Q19\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Conditional Diagramming\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"An editor is compiling a textbook containing essays by several different authors. The book will contain essays by Lind, Knight, or Jones, but it will not contain essays by all three. If the textbook contains an essay by Knight, then it will also contain an essay by Jones.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT125-S2-Q16\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Quantifiers\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"\\\\\\"Good hunter\\\\\\" and \\\\\\"bad hunter\\\\\\" are standard terms in the study of cats. Good hunters can kill prey that weigh up to half their body weight. All good hunters have a high muscle-to-fat ratio. Most wild cats are good hunters, but some domestic cats are good hunters as well.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT128-S3-Q13\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Sexual reproduction is achieved when the gamete of a male organism unites with the gamete of a female organism to form a new and genetically unique cell. Each of the two gametes contributes equally to the genetic material found in the new cell\'s nucleus. However, the genetic material found in the new cell\'s cytoplasm (the part of a cell outside the nucleus) is contributed exclusively by the female\'s gamete. A certain type of genetic material, referred to as GM62, is found only in cytoplasm.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT130-S3-Q10\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Conditional Diagramming\\",\\n      \\"Contrapositive Logic\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Only people who are willing to compromise should undergo mediation to resolve their conflicts. Actual litigation should be pursued only when one is sure that one\'s position is correct. People whose conflicts are based on ideology are unwilling to compromise.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT132-S2-Q6\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Causal Chains\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Although some nutritional facts about soft drinks are listed on their labels, exact caffeine content is not. Listing exact caffeine content would make it easier to limit, but not eliminate, one\'s caffeine intake. If it became easier for people to limit, but not eliminate, their caffeine intake, many people would do so, which would improve their health.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT132-S4-Q2\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Missing Link / Idea\\",\\n      \\"Sampling\\",\\n      \\"Principles in Answers\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"To discover what percentage of teenagers believe in telekinesis—the psychic ability to move objects without physically touching them—a recent survey asked a representative sample of teenagers whether they agreed with the following statement: \\\\\\"A person\'s thoughts can influence the movement of physical objects.\\\\\\" But because this statement is particularly ambiguous and is amenable to a naturalistic, uncontroversial interpretation, the survey\'s responses are also ambiguous.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT132-S4-Q6\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Contrapositive Logic\\",\\n      \\"Important Pivot\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"University administrator: Any proposal for a new department will not be funded if there are fewer than 50 people per year available for hire in that field and the proposed department would duplicate more than 25 percent of the material covered in one of our existing departments. The proposed Area Studies Department will duplicate more than 25 percent of the material covered in our existing Anthropology Department. However, we will fund the new department.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT134-S1-Q2\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"There are 70 to 100 Florida panthers alive today. This represents a very large increase over their numbers in the 1970s, but their population must reach at least 250 if it is to be self-sustaining. Their current habitat is not large enough to support any more of these animals, however.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT136-S2-Q14\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Quantifiers\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Only Canadian films are shown at the Lac Nichoutec Film Festival. This year, most of the films that won prizes at that festival also won prizes at international film festivals.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT137-S3-Q3\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Conditional Diagramming\\",\\n      \\"Missing Link / Idea\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Unless the building permit is obtained by February 1 of this year or some of the other activities necessary for construction of the new library can be completed in less time than originally planned, the new library will not be completed on schedule. It is now clear that the building permit cannot be obtained by February 1, so the new library will not be completed on schedule.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT138-S4-Q3\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Contrapositive Logic\\",\\n      \\"Conditional Diagramming\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"The level of sulfur dioxide in the atmosphere is slightly higher than it was ten years ago. This increase is troubling because ten years ago the Interior Ministry imposed new, stricter regulations on emissions from coal-burning power plants. If these regulations had been followed, then the level of sulfur dioxide in the atmosphere would have decreased.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT139-S1-Q10\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 1,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Science\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Large deposits of the rare mineral nahcolite formed in salty lakes 50 million to 52 million years ago during the Eocene epoch. Laboratory tests found that, in salty water, nahcolite can form only when the atmosphere contains at least 1,125 parts per million of carbon dioxide.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT142-S2-Q3\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Commentator: If a political administration is both\\\\neconomically successful and successful at\\\\nprotecting individual liberties, then it is an overall\\\\nsuccess. Even an administration that fails to care\\\\nfor the environment may succeed overall if it\\\\nprotects individual liberties. So far, the present\\\\nadministration has not cared for the environment\\\\nbut has successfully protected individual liberties.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT155-S1-Q6\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"EXCEPT\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"In a counterattack on the harmful effects of acid rain, wildlife experts are adding lime to the water of some lakes. “Liming” neutralizes the acid and thus staves off some damage caused by acid rain and restores the health of some lakes where life has already been harmed by acidification. Lakes in which lime is used must be treated periodically because their water is constantly being replaced, carrying away the lime. If a lake’s water is replaced more often than once every six months, it is not a candidate for liming because of the cost.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT155-S4-Q3\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 1,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Contrapositive Logic\\",\\n      \\"Science\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"Researcher: Newly formed neurons can help to heal an injured brain but only if they develop into the type of neurons that are most common in the injured area. Studies have shown that when a part of the brain called the striatum is injured, newly formed neurons in the striatum never become midsized spiny neurons, the type most common in the striatum.\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\"qid\\": \\"PT156-S4-Q7\\",\\n    \\"d\\": 2,\\n    \\"f\\": [\\n      \\"Dense Stimulus\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\"stimulus\\": \\"A truly visual art form—for example, painting—is one in\\\\n which time plays no essential role. Though it takes time\\\\n to look at a painting, there is no fixed order in which one\\\\n must look at its parts, and no fixed amount of time one\\\\n must spend examining it. In contrast, most art forms,\\\\n such as poetry and music, are essentially temporal; that\\\\n is, they require performance, which means they must\\\\n be experienced in a fixed order and over a roughly fixed\\\\n amount of time. Poetry, for instance, though often written\\\\n down and thus seemingly a visual art, actually must\\\\n be performed, even if the performance is only a silent\\\\n reading to oneself.\\"\\n  }\\n]\\n"}'}]

Returns granular flags so callers can surface partial-success\\r\\n * states truthfully (\\"Assigned, but email failed\\").\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Email is sent via the `send-assignment-email` edge function, which uses\\r\\n * Resend. If RESEND_API_KEY or FROM_EMAIL is unset the function returns\\r\\n * a truthful failure — we never fake email_ok.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface NotifyResult {\\r\\n  inbox_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  bell_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  email_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  inbox_error?: string;\\r\\n  bell_error?: string;\\r\\n  email_error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface NotifyParams {\\r\\n  adminUserId: string;\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  /** Path the student should land on, e.g. /classroom/item/<id> */\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n  /** Short body shown in the inbox message + bell notification (optional). */\\r\\n  description?: string;\\r\\n  /** Type label used in the email body, e.g. \\"homework\\", \\"bootcamp_module\\". */\\r\\n  assignmentType?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function findOrCreateThread(adminUserId: string, studentId: string): Promise<string> {\\r\\n  const { data: myParts, error: myPartsErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n    .eq(\\"user_id\\", adminUserId);\\r\\n  if (myPartsErr) throw myPartsErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const myConvIds = (myParts ?? []).map((p) => p.conversation_id);\\r\\n  if (myConvIds.length > 0) {\\r\\n    const { data: shared, error: sharedErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n      .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n      .eq(\\"user_id\\", studentId)\\r\\n      .in(\\"conversation_id\\", myConvIds)\\r\\n      .limit(1);\\r\\n    if (sharedErr) throw sharedErr;\\r\\n    if (shared && shared.length > 0) return shared[0].conversation_id;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: conv, error: cErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversations\\")\\r\\n    .insert({ subject: null, created_by: adminUserId })\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .single();\\r\\n  if (cErr || !conv) throw cErr ?? new Error(\\"Failed to create conversation\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { error: pErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .insert([\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: adminUserId },\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: studentId },\\r\\n    ]);\\r\\n  if (pErr) throw pErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return conv.id;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function notifyStudentOfClassroomItem(\\r\\n  params: NotifyParams,\\r\\n): Promise<NotifyResult> {\\r\\n  const { adminUserId, studentId, title, route, description, assignmentType } = params;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const result: NotifyResult = {\\r\\n    inbox_ok: false,\\r\\n    bell_ok: false,\\r\\n    email_ok: false,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(adminUserId, studentId);\\r\\n    const body =\\r\\n      `New assignment: ${title}\\\\n\\\\n` +\\r\\n      (description ? `${description}\\\\n\\\\n` : \\"\\") +\\r\\n      `View in your Classroom: ${route}`;\\r\\n    const { error: mErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"messages\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        conversation_id: conversationId,\\r\\n        sender_id: adminUserId,\\r\\n        body,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (mErr) throw mErr;\\r\\n    result.inbox_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:inbox] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"inbox failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { error: nErr } = await (supabase as unknown as {\\r\\n      from: (t: string) => {\\r\\n        insert: (v: unknown) => Promise<{ error: { message: string } | null }>;\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    })\\r\\n      .from(\\"notifications\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        user_id: studentId,\\r\\n        type: \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n        title: `New assignment: ${title}`,\\r\\n        body: description ?? null,\\r\\n        link: route,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (nErr) throw nErr;\\r\\n    result.bell_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:bell] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.bell_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"bell failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Email step. Failure is recorded but the assignment is not affected.\\r\\n  const emailRes = await sendAssignmentEmail({\\r\\n    studentId,\\r\\n    title,\\r\\n    description,\\r\\n    route,\\r\\n    assignmentType: assignmentType ?? \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  result.email_ok = emailRes.ok;\\r\\n  if (!emailRes.ok) result.email_error = emailRes.error;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n"}'}]

Date(item.unlock_at).toISOString() !== expectedUnlock) throw new Error('Local calendar dates differ');\n+  const notices = await Marks finished tasks done (struck through) in the task ledger, refreshes the handoff cache, and appends to the log. Trigger with /wrapup.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nClose the current LSAT U / Aspiring Attorneys session. Leave the next session a clean, accurate handoff and mark completed work as done so it is never re-run. Resolve the vault root from the current workspace and its nearest instruction files; never assume a saved `~/ai-os/`, Windows, or Mac path. In this vault, read `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` and obey its index/log conventions.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 0: read the anchor\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore writing anything, re-read `wiki/tasks.md` and `wiki/hot.md` so the handoff reflects the real goal, not just the last thing touched. If a `plans/` file or README framed this session, skim it too.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 1: confirm task status changes only when needed\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom verified session work, determine whether task status changes are needed. If no task status changes are needed, leave `wiki/tasks.md` alone and continue with the authorized handoff and log updates without a confirmation step. If task status changes are proposed, show Joshua the concrete proposal before editing `wiki/tasks.md`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nProposing for wiki/tasks.md:\\r\\n  -> Done (strike through): <items you believe are finished this session>\\r\\n  -> Stays in Now: <still in progress>\\r\\n  -> New Next: <anything surfaced that should be queued>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWait for Joshua to approve or correct proposed task status changes, unless those exact changes were already approved in this session. Completion MUST be correct: a wrong \\"Done\\" is the exact failure this prevents. Do not mark something done on a guess; if unsure, leave its status unchanged and say so. This gate applies to task status changes, not to routine authorized handoff/log filing. If requesting approval, explain that this skill requires confirmation before changing task status.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 2: write the authorized handoff and log\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **`wiki/tasks.md`** - move approved finished items into `## Done`, wrapping each in `~~...~~` with a date, e.g. `~~Wire AI tutor into official drill surface~~ (2026-06-12)`. Update `## Now` and `## Next` only as approved. Preserve older task records; do not prune or delete them without explicit authorization. Skip this file when no task changes were approved or needed.\\r\\n2. **`wiki/hot.md`** - rewrite the `## Most recent (YYYY-MM-DD): <title>` block with 4 to 8 bullets of what changed. Refresh `## Active priorities` and `## Open threads` so they are true now.\\r\\n3. **`wiki/log.md`** - append a new entry at the BOTTOM of the log using `## [YYYY-MM-DD] system | Session wrapup`. Include source/output links, meaningful changed pages, verified results, unresolved work, and any skipped steps. Use Joshua\'s client date/time context. Preserve old entries exactly, including their legacy formats; never rewrite or reorder them. Multiple sessions per day are normal, so add a distinct title suffix when useful. If this session\'s authorized handoff and log entry already contain the same verified work, report them rather than duplicating the entry.\\r\\n4. **Task anchor** - if a README, plan, or wiki page advanced, make a surgical status update. Touch only what changed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 3: confirm, then stop\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nHanded off: <1 line headline of this session>\\r\\nTasks: <N marked done, M still in Now>\\r\\nLogged: <the wiki/log.md entry label you added>\\r\\nNext session starts at: <the top open item in tasks.md / hot.md>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes. Terse. Surgical changes only (Karpathy: touch only what changed).\\r\\n- `raw/` is immutable; never write there.\\r\\n- `pickup` is the mirror skill for session start; run it only when explicitly requested.\\r\\n- Read current target contents before writing. Preserve unrelated changes. text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/system/scripts/verify-guided-spec.mjs\n@@\n for (const file of files) {\n+  const section = /^PT(\\d+)\\.S(\\d+)@(\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2})$/.exec(file);\n+  if (section) {\n+    const [, pt, number, day] = section;\n+    const title = `PT${pt}.S${number} (LR)`;\n+    const sets = await read(`rest/v1/homework_sets?title=eq.${encodeURIComponent(title)}&select=id,title,question_qids,estimated_minutes`);\n+    if (sets.length !== 1) throw new Error('Expected one section set');\n+    const set = sets[0];\n+    const raw = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(projectPath('lsatu', `public/data/PT${pt}-S${number}-LR.json`), 'utf8'));\n+    const expected = raw.filter(q => q.practiceTest === `PT${pt}` && q.sectionNumber === Number(number)).sort((a, b) => a.questionNumber - b.questionNumber).map(q => `PT${pt}-S${number}-Q${q.questionNumber}`);\n+    if (!isDeepStrictEqual(set.question_qids, expected) || set.estimated_minutes !== 35) throw new Error('Section content or timing differs');\n+    const rows = await read(`rest/v1/homework_assignments?student_id=eq.${student.class_id}&set_id=eq.${set.id}&select=id,status,question_qids`);\n+    if (rows.length !== 1 || !isDeepStrictEqual(rows[0].question_qids, expected)) throw new Error('Section assignment differs');\n+    const items = await read(`rest/v1/classroom_items?source_id=eq.${rows[0].id}&student_id=eq.${student.class_id}&select=id,visible_to_student,status,unlock_at,due_at`);\n+    if (items.length !== 1 || !items[0].visible_to_student || items[0].status === 'archived') throw new Error('Section visibility failed');\n+    if (new Date(items[0].unlock_at).toISOString() !== new Date(`${day}T00:00:00`).toISOString() || new Date(items[0].due_at).toISOString() !== new Date(`${day}T23:59:59`).toISOString()) throw new Error('Section dates differ');\n+    console.log(JSON.stringify({ day, title, assignment_id: rows[0].id, classroom_item_id: items[0].id, visible: true, exact_content: true, count: expected.length, minutes: 35 }));\n+    continue;\n+  }\n   const spec = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.resolve(file), 'utf8'));\n*** End Patch"));

A router over the skills in this repo.\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Ask Matt\\r\\n\\r\\nYou don\'t remember every skill, so ask.\\r\\n\\r\\nA **flow** is a path through the skills. Most paths run along one **main flow**, and two **on-ramps** merge onto it. Everything else is standalone, or a vocabulary layer that runs underneath.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The main flow: idea → ship\\r\\n\\r\\nThe route most work travels. You have an idea and want it built.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens the idea by interview. Start here whenever you are **working in a working directory**: it\'s stateful, retaining what it learns in `GLOSSARY.md` and ADRs. (No working directory? Use `/grill-me` instead, covered under Standalone. Both run the same `/grilling` primitive; `grill-with-docs` is the one that leaves a paper trail, which makes it the better of the two whenever a repo is there to leave it in.)\\r\\n2. **Branch: can you settle every question in conversation?** If a question needs a runnable answer (state, business logic, a UI you have to see), detour through a prototype, bridged by **`/handoff`** in both directions (a prototype lives in its own directory, which is exactly what `/handoff` is for; see Phase boundaries):\\r\\n   - **`/handoff`** out, then open a fresh session against that file,\\r\\n   - **`/prototype`** to answer the question with throwaway code,\\r\\n   - **`/handoff`** back what you learned, and reference it from the original idea thread.\\r\\n3. **Branch: is this a multi-session build?**\\r\\n   - **Yes** → **`/to-spec`** (turn the thread into a spec), then **`/to-tickets`** to split it into tracer-bullet tickets, each declaring its **blocking edges**. Then work the tickets one of two ways:\\r\\n     - **`/implement`** per ticket, **`/clear`ing context between each one**. On a local tracker that\'s one file per ticket under `.scratch/<feature>/issues/`, worked blockers-first by hand; on a real tracker the edges become native blocking links, so any ticket whose blockers are done can be grabbed. Each ticket is self-contained, so the last one\'s context is disposable.\\r\\n     - **`/implement-spec`** for the whole spec in one run. It reads the tickets as a **task graph**, runs implementer subagents across the ready **frontier** in parallel, and lands everything on one **integration branch**. Reach for it when you\'d rather orchestrate the build than drive each ticket yourself.\\r\\n   - **No** → **`/implement`** right here, in the same context window.\\r\\n\\r\\n   Either way, the code gets built by driving **`/tdd`** (one red-green slice at a time) and closes out with **`/code-review`**, a two-axis review (Standards + Spec) of the diff. `/implement` runs both per ticket; `/implement-spec`\'s implementers each drive `/tdd`, and it runs one `/code-review` over the integration branch. Reach for **`/tdd`** on its own when you just want to build a concrete behaviour test-first without a full spec, and **`/code-review`** on its own whenever you want to review a branch or PR against a fixed point.\\r\\n\\r\\n   When the work goes up as a pull request, **`/pr`** shapes the body: the smallest visual that shows the change, before/after evidence that it works, and a one-way or two-way door call. It\'s model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it whenever it writes a PR.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **`/retro`** closes the loop. After a build, and especially one that went sideways, it looks back over the session and suggests changes to the agent\'s **environment**, not the code: navigation pointers, automated checks, the coding standards `/code-review` enforces, steering files, tooling. Mechanical mistakes become deterministic checks; judgement calls become coding standards. The next build then starts from a better environment.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Context hygiene\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep steps 1–3 in **one unbroken context window** (don\'t compact or clear until after `/to-tickets`) so the grilling, spec, and tickets all build on the same thinking. Each `/implement` then starts fresh, working from the ticket. Run `/retro` in the session it\'s looking back on, before you clear; after clearing, point it at that session\'s log instead.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe limit on this is the **[smart zone](https://www.aihero.dev/ai-coding-dictionary/smart-zone)**: the window (~150k tokens on state-of-the-art models) within which the model still reasons sharply. If a session approaches it before `/to-tickets`, don\'t push on degraded; `/compact` at the nearest phase boundary and carry on (see Phase boundaries).\\r\\n\\r\\n## On-ramps\\r\\n\\r\\nA starting situation that generates work, then merges onto the main flow.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Bugs and requests piling up** → **`/triage`**. It moves issues through triage roles and produces agent-ready issues, which **`/implement`** later picks up.\\r\\n\\r\\n  Triage is only for issues **you didn\'t create**: bug reports, incoming feature requests, anything that arrives raw. Tickets that `/to-tickets` produced are already agent-ready, so **don\'t triage them**.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Something\'s broken** → **`/diagnosing-bugs`**. For the hard ones: the bug that resists a first glance, the intermittent flake, the regression that crept in between two known-good states. It refuses to theorise until it has a **tight feedback loop** (one command that already goes red on *this* bug), then fixes with a regression test. Its post-mortem hands off to **`/improve-codebase-architecture`** when the real finding is that there\'s no good seam to lock the bug down.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **A huge, foggy effort: a greenfield project or a huge feature build, too big for one session** → **`/wayfinder`**, the most cognitively demanding flow here. When the way from here to the destination isn\'t visible yet, it charts a **shared map** of **decision tickets** on the issue tracker and resolves them one at a time, producing **decisions, not deliverables**, until the fog is pushed back and the way is clear. Where **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens an idea you can hold in one session, wayfinder is for the idea you can\'t, and it\'s slower and denser, so save it for exactly that, never a well-scoped feature.\\r\\n\\r\\n  When the map clears, **it hands off, it doesn\'t build**: merge onto the main flow at **`/to-spec`**, which collapses the map\'s linked decisions into a buildable plan, then `/to-tickets` and `/implement` as usual. Looping the map straight into `/implement` skips that collapse and throws the linked detail away, so go straight to `/implement` only when the effort turned out genuinely small.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codebase health\\r\\n\\r\\nNot feature work, just upkeep.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`/improve-codebase-architecture`** runs whenever you have a spare moment to keep the codebase good for agents to operate in. It surfaces **deepening opportunities**; picking one _generates an idea_ you can take into the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`. It\'s the survey that finds the candidates; **`/codebase-design`** (below) is the bench you design the chosen one on.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Vocabulary underneath\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo model-invoked references that run *beneath* the other skills, each the single source of truth for its vocabulary. Reach for them directly when the **words**, not the process, are the problem; or let the skills above pull them in.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`/domain-modeling`**: sharpen the project\'s *domain* language: challenge a fuzzy term, resolve an overloaded word (\\"account\\" doing three jobs), record a hard-to-reverse decision as an ADR. It\'s the active discipline `/grill-with-docs` drives to keep `GLOSSARY.md` a clean glossary.\\r\\n- **`/codebase-design`** is the deep-module vocabulary (module, interface, depth, seam, adapter, leverage, locality) for designing a module\'s *shape*: a lot of behaviour behind a small interface at a clean seam. `/tdd` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` both speak it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\nA **phase** is a chunk of work inside a session: the grilling, the implementation, the QA. At the **boundary** between two of them you have five options, and picking between them is the fuzziest decision in this whole map:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Continue**: stay put. Costs nothing, loses nothing.\\r\\n- **`/clear`**: empty the window, when nothing here matters to what\'s next.\\r\\n- **`/handoff`** writes a portable markdown file. Narrow: only for a **new harness**, a **new directory**, a **colleague**, or forking a side task **mid-phase**. What it buys is portability.\\r\\n- **Subagent**: send a tightly-scoped task to its own window and get a report back.\\r\\n- **`/compact`** compresses this context and seeds a fresh session with it. The **default**, at the bottom of the tree rather than the first reach.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead [PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md](PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md) for the ordered tree: the five questions, the reasoning behind each branch, and why the primary-source cost makes **Continue** the one to rule out first. Make the decision **at** a boundary; mid-phase, continue or split the rest into subagents.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standalone\\r\\n\\r\\nOff the main flow entirely.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`/grill-me`**: the same relentless interview as `/grill-with-docs`, but **stateless**: it saves nothing locally and builds no `GLOSSARY.md`. Reach for it when you are **not working in a working directory** (sharpening a plan, a design, a piece of writing, anything with no repo under it). If you are in a working directory, use `/grill-with-docs` instead: it runs the same interview and leaves a paper trail, so it is strictly the better one.\\r\\n- **`/grilling`** is the interview primitive itself: rounds, the frontier, facts are the agent\'s job and decisions are yours. `/grill-me` and `/grill-with-docs` are the two named ways in, and `/triage`, `/wayfinder` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` all run it internally. Reach for it directly only when you want the interview with no wrapper around it.\\r\\n- **`/prototype`** is a small, throwaway program that answers one design question: does this state model feel right, or what should this UI look like. Throwaway is a constraint on how the code is written, not a promise to destroy it: the answer folds into the real code, and the prototype itself is kept as a **primary source** on a `prototype/<name>` branch out of main, pointed at from the implementation issue. It\'s the detour in step 2 of the main flow, but reach for it any time a design question is hard to settle on paper.\\r\\n- **`/research`**: delegate reading legwork to a **background agent**: it investigates a question against **primary sources**, then leaves a cited Markdown file in the repo. Keep working while it reads. The file it produces is something to take *into* the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`, since research feeds the thinking rather than replacing it.\\r\\n- **`/to-questionnaire`** comes in when the thing blocking you isn\'t in your head or the codebase but in **someone else\'s**, and it writes them a questionnaire to fill in. It\'s the inverse of `/grill-me`: instead of interviewing you about the subject, it interviews you about the **send** (who it\'s going to, what you need back) and aims the questions at the gap. What comes back is material for `/grill-with-docs` or `/to-spec`.\\r\\n- **`/wizard`** is for the steps only a **human** can take: provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, clicking through an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, running a one-off migration or cutover. It generates an interactive bash script that opens each URL, captures each value, and writes it into `.env` and GitHub secrets, so the procedure stops being something you re-explain to an agent every time. Model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it the moment it hits a wall only you can pass. If the agent could just do it itself, it should; this is for where a human is genuinely in the loop.\\r\\n- **`/wait-what`** is the corrective for a message that didn\'t land. Use it mid-conversation, inside any other skill, and the agent re-pitches what it just said with the context you were missing, in plain English, using the `GLOSSARY.md` vocabulary. It works after the fact; `/grill-with-docs` is the upfront cure, because a shared language agreed early is what stops the jargon arriving at all.\\r\\n- **`/teach`**: learn a concept over multiple sessions, using the current directory as a stateful workspace.\\r\\n- **`/writing-for-agents`** is the reference for writing documents agents consume: skills, AGENTS.md, pointed-at docs.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Precondition\\r\\n\\r\\n**`/setup-matt-pocock-skills`**: run before your first engineering flow to configure the issue tracker, triage labels, and doc layout the other skills assume. Templates always leave the \\"paying student\\" defaults on, the\\r\\nsame as the dashboard does.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Go without asking:** every read, `access` grants for one named student,\\r\\n`due`, and `read`. **Confirm first:** `archive`, `unarchive`, `unassign`,\\r\\n`--all-off`, and any `--revoke`, because each of these takes something away\\r\\nfrom a real student. **Not available here on purpose:** deleting a student\\r\\n(permanent), changing admin roles, and anything that edits app code or the\\r\\ndatabase schema. Those stay with Joshua in the dashboard or Lovable.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Other platform jobs that already have a home:** one-off booking links and\\r\\nnew passes go through the `booking-link` skill (`scripts/booking-link.ts`).\\r\\nAccommodations emails use the `accommodations-email` skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nAdd `--json` to any read when you want to compute on the output. `overdue` and\\r\\n`quiet` include former students, because the platform has no \\"former\\" flag.\\r\\nCross-check `students/roster.generated.md` before acting on either list.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen you are unsure what a student already has, run `status` before\\r\\nassigning. One call catches both double-assigns and the overdue pile.\\r\\n\\r\\nFlags: `--schedule YYYY-MM-DD` unlocks the work that morning. `--due YYYY-MM-DD`\\r\\nis due at the end of that local day and shows on the card and the calendar\\r\\nstrip. `--dry-run` resolves the student and the work and writes nothing — use it\\r\\nwhen the name is at all uncertain.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Dates are absolute.** \\"Due Saturday\\" means look up the date of the coming\\r\\nSaturday and pass that. On 2026-09-10 (a Thursday) Saturday was 2026-09-12; a\\r\\nhandoff once carried 09-13, which is the Sunday.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo pace a student over several days, run the command once per day with a\\r\\n`--schedule` on each. Each run is its own assignment, its own row, and its own\\r\\nemail, so she is told about each day rather than handed a block.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery assignment lands on the home calendar strip and the cell opens that\\r\\nassignment — scheduled work on its unlock day, unscheduled work on the day you\\r\\nassigned it. Nothing needs to be written by hand to put work on the calendar;\\r\\nif an assignment is missing from the band, that is a bug to fix in\\r\\n`useClassroomHome`, not something to patch into `classroom_items`.\\r\\n\\r\\nAlways `--dry-run` first for a student you have not assigned to before in this\\r\\nsession. It costs one second and it is the only cheap way to catch a wrong\\r\\naccount.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why a script and not a direct DB write\\r\\n\\r\\n`scripts/assign.ts` calls the app\'s **own** `assignWork()` from\\r\\n`src/lib/assignWork.ts`. Assigning touches seven tables — the assignment\\r\\nsnapshot, `classroom_items`, the conversation and its participants, `messages`,\\r\\n`notifications` — plus the email edge function, and it rolls the whole thing\\r\\nback if the classroom item or notify throws. There is no RPC and no edge\\r\\nfunction for it; it is client-side code.\\r\\n\\r\\nSo never hand-write these rows. A second implementation drifts from the UI the\\r\\nfirst time the app changes, and produces assignments subtly unlike the ones\\r\\nJoshua makes by clicking.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Credentials\\r\\n\\r\\nRead from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is **not** a git repo:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`, `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` — already set\\r\\n\\r\\nNever put a credential in `projects/lsatu/.env`. That file **is tracked in git**\\r\\nand would be committed. The script signs in as Joshua and works under normal\\r\\nadmin RLS, the same permission path the UI uses.\\r\\n\\r\\n`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is deliberately **not** used even when present: it\\r\\nbypasses RLS on every write, so a script running as service_role would hide an\\r\\nRLS regression instead of hitting it. The script warns and falls back.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Expected output\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nAssign \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\" → Celia <celiacutler@gmail.com>\\r\\nAssigned. id=e83e2193-…\\r\\n  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nA notify channel reading `FAILED` is **not** an assignment failure — the work is\\r\\nin her Classroom and only a message went missing. Report it, do not re-run.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Failure modes\\r\\n\\r\\n**`duplicate key … activity_assignments_active_unique`** — she already holds an\\r\\nactive assignment for this Activity. Not a bug; the DB refusing a double-assign.\\r\\nCheck what she has before re-trying (below).\\r\\n\\r\\n**`\\"X\\" is ambiguous`** — the script lists every matching account and stops.\\r\\nPick the full name or the email. Never guess: a misdirected assignment is\\r\\nsilent, because she simply never sees it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Sign-in failed** — the credentials in `~/ai-os/.env` are wrong or the account\\r\\ngained MFA.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Checking what a student already has\\r\\n\\r\\n`system/scripts/lsatu status \\"Celia\\"` lists every open classroom item with its\\r\\ntype, status, assign date and due date, and flags anything overdue.\\r\\n\\r\\n## A PT section as timed homework\\r\\n\\r\\n`--section PT156.S2` (also accepts `PT156-S2`, `pt 156 s 2`) assigns one\\r\\nLogical Reasoning section to run under the real 35:00 section clock, with the\\r\\nofficial PrepTest header, exactly like a section inside a practice test.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat the script does, verified end to end on 2026-09-10 on\\r\\n`joshua.fisseha01@gmail.com` (the test account):\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Reads `public/data/PT156-S2-LR.json` and builds the qids\\r\\n   `PT156-S2-Q1 … Q25` in order. No file → it lists the sections that PT does\\r\\n   have and stops. Only LR sections are loaded; there is no RC/LG file set.\\r\\n2. Finds or creates the homework set titled **`PT156.S2 (LR)`** with exactly\\r\\n   those qids and `estimated_minutes = 35`. One set per section, reused; a\\r\\n   same-title set with different questions stops the run.\\r\\n3. Calls the app\'s own `assignWork({ kind: \'homework_set\' })`: assignment row,\\r\\n   classroom item (with `due_at`), inbox message, bell, email.\\r\\n4. Turns on `has_classroom_access` and `has_drill_access` if either was off,\\r\\n   because the set opens at `/classroom/:id` and runs at `/drill`.\\r\\n\\r\\nExpected output:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nAssign \\"PT156.S2 (LR)\\" → josh <joshua.fisseha01@gmail.com>  (due 2026-09-13)\\r\\n  25 questions, timed 35:00 · set reused (ff0fe127-…)\\r\\nAssigned. id=04e154c8-…\\r\\n  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Already assigned:** the script says\\r\\n`already has \\"PT156.S2 (LR)\\" (in_progress, assigned 2026-09-10)` and stops. A\\r\\nset can be re-issued only after that copy is `completed`. Do not work around\\r\\nit by making a second set.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why the timer is real:** `src/lib/drillRunSpec.ts` `singleSectionOf()` sees\\r\\nthat every qid is one PT+section and runs it as `full-section` (35-minute\\r\\ncountdown) instead of `type-drill` (a stopwatch). Shipped with `--section` in\\r\\nPR #113, merged 2026-09-11.\\r\\n\\r\\n`--set \\"<title or id>\\"` assigns any existing homework set, timed or not, with\\r\\nthe same guards.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Read, Recall, Colour specifically\\r\\n\\r\\nAssign the title **\\"Read, Recall, Colour\\"** — the section-library door. She then\\r\\npicks any of 12 seeded sections (PT1.S3, PT21.S3, PT101.S2, PT102.S3, PT103.S1,\\r\\nPT104.S1, PT105.S1, PT105.S2, PT106.S2, PT112.S4, PT134.S3, PT140.S1), 25 min\\r\\neach. Every attempt is kept; re-running saves a new attempt.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe script prefers the exact title, so it resolves to the door rather than one\\r\\nof the 12 sections. Pass a `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` slug only to pin her to\\r\\none section deliberately.\\r\\n\\r\\n## One headless backend, never Lovable for content\\r\\n\\r\\nDecided by Joshua on 2026-09-23 and refined 2026-09-29. `system/scripts/lsatu`\\r\\nis the headless path for work assignments, messaging, and student data. The\\r\\nlocal `lsat-u-lessons` MCP wraps `system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs post-lesson`\\r\\nfor interactive lesson assignment and exact HTML verification. It is a local\\r\\nwrapper over the same authenticated backend, not a hosted connector or an app\\r\\ndeploy. Lovable is only for changing the app\'s own code.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The hosted connector (MCP): exists, not used\\r\\n\\r\\nLSAT U also runs a real MCP server, `lsat-u-mcp` v0.3.0, at\\r\\n`https://atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju.supabase.co/functions/v1/mcp`. Verified live on\\r\\n2026-09-23: it returns 401 without a token and serves OAuth discovery. The\\r\\nsource is `src/lib/mcp/` in the app repo, deployed as the `mcp` edge function.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Connect:** claude.ai → Settings → Connectors → add a custom connector with\\r\\n  that URL, then sign in with an LSAT U account. There is no key to paste.\\r\\n- **Who gets the admin tools:** any account with the `admin` role in\\r\\n  `user_roles`. To grant someone access, give their account that role; remove\\r\\n  the role to revoke it. Students who connect see only their own data.\\r\\n- **Tools today:** `find_students`, `list_students`, `list_homework_sets`,\\r\\n  `assign_homework`, `assign_passage_map_homework`, `post_classroom_note`,\\r\\n  `send_note`, `get_assignment_status`, plus the student-side `get_my_profile`,\\r\\n  `list_my_homework` and `list_my_classroom_items`.\\r\\n- **In the CLI but not the connector:** activities, guided drills, bootcamps,\\r\\n  timed `--section` with due dates, and every analytics read. Adding them\\r\\n  means app code plus a Lovable deploy, which needs Joshua\'s credit\\r\\n  confirmation first.\\r\\n\\r\\nOn this laptop, use the CLI: it does more and costs nothing. The connector is\\r\\nfor when Joshua is away from the laptop, or when someone else needs access.\\r\\n\\r\\nRunning `vite-node` inside the app repo rewrites\\r\\n`supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` with a local path (the `@lovable.dev/mcp-js`\\r\\nVite plugin). Never commit that change. The wrapper restores the file after\\r\\neach run.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Related\\r\\n\\r\\nVault: `wiki/ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY.md` (the browser path and what fires),\\r\\n`wiki/STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP.md` (which account is which student —\\r\\n33 platform accounts, 20 real students, 13 unmatched).\\r\\n"}'}]

Should an existing rule be removed or clarified? Classify the violation first: a **mechanical** one (a fixed syntactic pattern, a banned API, an import shape, a file-location rule) gets a deterministic check, full stop: a custom rule in the repo\'s own linter, a new pre-commit hook, or a new CI job, whichever the repo\'s language and existing guardrail make cheapest. Default to building the check over writing the rule. Reserve `CODING_STANDARDS.md` for genuine **judgement calls** (cross-file consistency, \\"matches the surrounding style,\\" anything no guardrail could ever substitute for). _Use when_ the reviewer agent failed to catch a mistake.\\r\\n- **Global AGENTS.md**: are there any steering instructions that should be moved to coding standards (or automated checks) instead? _Use when_ the AGENTS.md file is particularly large - in the repo OR the user\'s global scope.\\r\\n- **Tool economy**: did the agent make expensive tool calls that could be streamlined? Is there any custom tooling (CLI\'s, MCP\'s) that is particularly token-inefficient? _Use when_ the agent made an expensive tool call.\\r\\n- **No-ops**: look for instructions in steering files that don\'t modify the agent\'s behavior. _Use when_ the steering files are large and unwieldy.\\r\\n- **Information access**: look for opportunities to increase the agent\'s access to information. Present these candidates to the user, in order of severity.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Reference\\r\\n\\r\\n### Implementation vs Review\\r\\n\\r\\nRemember that all work goes through two stages: implementation and review. The implementation agent has the most **context pressure**. They are responsible for exploration, writing code, and debugging failures.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe review agent has the least context pressure - it receives a diff, so no exploration needed. It often does not need to write code or debug.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis means that the review agent should be responsible for imposing coding standards, not the implementation agent.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Files\\r\\n\\r\\nYou have access to several files in the repo:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`: these files are pushed to the context window of any agent working in this repo. They should be used incredibly sparingly, usually only for **navigation pointers** to other files.\\r\\n- `CODING_STANDARDS.md`: this file is read during review, not implementation. Add **navigation pointers** to docs folders if the standards file gets more than 1,000 lines long.\\r\\n- Docs: use docs as references files, pointed to by other files. Look for existing docs before writing new ones.\\r\\n- Skills: use skills for docs (since their description goes into the agent\'s context window), or for user-invoked commands. One causation\\r\\n * drill therefore serves a beginner and an advanced student with different\\r\\n * questions, which is the whole point.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Pure on purpose: no supabase, no question bank singleton, injectable rng.\\r\\n * The wrapper in guidedDrills.ts supplies the pool.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Why the resolution is not a clean in-band draw. Defaults to Math.random. */\\r\\n  rng?: () => number;\\r\\n\\r\\n  /*\\r\\n   * Percentage-aware selection. Every field below is optional and every\\r\\n   * default is inert: with `stats` absent or both weights at 0, this function\\r\\n   * is byte-identical to the version that had none of them.\\r\\n   * `guidedDrillLevel.parity.test.ts` is the proof, and it was recorded before\\r\\n   * any of this existed.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n\\r\\n  /** Cohort measurements by qid. The caller passes the map; this never fetches. */\\r\\n  stats?: ReadonlyMap<string, QuestionStat> | null;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * How much the cohort percentage moves a question\'s difficulty for the\\r\\n   * purpose of BAND MEMBERSHIP. 0 uses the human label alone.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  empiricalWeight?: number;\\r\\n  /** Trap taxonomy tags this student keeps falling for. */\\r\\n  targetTrapTags?: readonly string[] | null;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * How much trap targeting orders the in-band group, from 0 (pure shuffle,\\r\\n   * today\'s behaviour) to 1 (pure targeting, no jitter left). It trades\\r\\n   * freshness for aim, which is why it is a dial and not a switch.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  trapWeight?: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LeveledDrillResolution {\\r\\n  qids: string[];\\r\\n  /** \'pinned\' when an exact list was used, \'pool\' when questions were drawn. */\\r\\n  source: \'pinned\' | \'pool\';\\r\\n  level: StudentLevel | null;\\r\\n  band: DifficultyBand | null;\\r\\n  poolCount: number;\\r\\n  /** How many of the pool sit inside the student\'s band. */\\r\\n  inBandCount: number;\\r\\n  /** How many of the returned questions had to come from outside the band. */\\r\\n  outOfBandUsed: number;\\r\\n  warning: LeveledDrillWarning | null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  /** How many of the returned questions have cohort data. null with no stats. */\\r\\n  statsCoverage: { measured: number; total: number } | null;\\r\\n  /** Mean cohort accuracy of the returned set. LOWER IS HARDER. */\\r\\n  meanCorrectPct: number | null;\\r\\n  /** How many of the returned questions lean on one of the target traps. */\\r\\n  trapMatches: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Fisher-Yates. The existing runtime used `sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5)`,\\r\\n * which is a biased shuffle and not seedable; this is neither.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction shuffle<T>(items: T[], rng: () => number): T[] {\\r\\n  const out = [...items];\\r\\n  for (let i = out.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {\\r\\n    const j = Math.floor(rng() * (i + 1));\\r\\n    [out[i], out[j]] = [out[j], out[i]];\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Drop unknown and duplicate qids, preserving the authored order. */\\r\\nfunction cleanQids(qids: readonly string[], knownQids?: Set<string>): string[] {\\r\\n  const seen = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const out: string[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const qid of qids) {\\r\\n    if (!qid || seen.has(qid)) continue;\\r\\n    if (knownQids && knownQids.size > 0 && !knownQids.has(qid)) continue;\\r\\n    seen.add(qid);\\r\\n    out.push(qid);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * What the returned set looks like in cohort terms. Reporting only: it never\\r\\n * changes which questions were chosen, so it is safe to compute on every path.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction describeSet(\\r\\n  qids: readonly string[],\\r\\n  stats: ReadonlyMap<string, QuestionStat> | null | undefined,\\r\\n  targets: readonly string[] | null | undefined,\\r\\n): Pick<LeveledDrillResolution, \'statsCoverage\' | \'meanCorrectPct\' | \'trapMatches\'> {\\r\\n  if (!stats || stats.size === 0) {\\r\\n    return { statsCoverage: null, meanCorrectPct: null, trapMatches: 0 };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  let measured = 0;\\r\\n  let total = 0;\\r\\n  let trapMatches = 0;\\r\\n  for (const qid of qids) {\\r\\n    const stat = stats.get(qid);\\r\\n    if (!stat) continue;\\r\\n    measured += 1;\\r\\n    total += stat.correctPct;\\r\\n    if (trapScore(stat, targets) > 0) trapMatches += 1;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    statsCoverage: { measured, total: qids.length },\\r\\n    meanCorrectPct: measured > 0 ? Math.round((total / measured) * 10) / 10 : null,\\r\\n    trapMatches,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function resolveLeveledDrillQids(input: LeveledDrillInput): LeveledDrillResolution {\\r\\n  const {\\r\\n    pool,\\r\\n    questionCount,\\r\\n    drillQids,\\r\\n    assignmentQids,\\r\\n    knownQids,\\r\\n    level,\\r\\n    rng = Math.random,\\r\\n    stats = null,\\r\\n    empiricalWeight = 0,\\r\\n    targetTrapTags = null,\\r\\n    trapWeight = 0,\\r\\n  } = input;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const band = level ? levelBand(level) : null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  // 1. This\\r\\n  // branch exists for hand-pinned assignments.)\\r\\n  const pinned = cleanQids(assignmentQids ?? drillQids ?? [], knownQids);\\r\\n  if (pinned.length > 0) {\\r\\n    const anyOutOfBand =\\r\\n      band != null &&\\r\\n      pool.some((q) => pinned.includes(q.qid) && !isInBand(q.difficulty, band));\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      qids: pinned,\\r\\n      source: \'pinned\',\\r\\n      level,\\r\\n      band,\\r\\n      poolCount: pool.length,\\r\\n      inBandCount: band ? pool.filter((q) => isInBand(q.difficulty, band)).length : pool.length,\\r\\n      outOfBandUsed: 0,\\r\\n      warning: band && anyOutOfBand ? \'pinned_level_not_applied\' : null,\\r\\n      ...describeSet(pinned, stats, targetTrapTags),\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // 2. Draw from the pool. `want` reproduces the existing runtime rule.\\r\\n  const want = questionCount ?? Math.min(pool.length, 10);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (pool.length === 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      qids: [],\\r\\n      source: \'pool\',\\r\\n      level,\\r\\n      band,\\r\\n      poolCount: 0,\\r\\n      inBandCount: 0,\\r\\n      outOfBandUsed: 0,\\r\\n      warning: \'empty\',\\r\\n      statsCoverage: null,\\r\\n      meanCorrectPct: null,\\r\\n      trapMatches: 0,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // No level: byte-identical to the behaviour before levels existed.\\r\\n  if (!band) {\\r\\n    const qids = shuffle(pool, rng).slice(0, want).map((q) => q.qid);\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      qids,\\r\\n      source: \'pool\',\\r\\n      level: null,\\r\\n      band: null,\\r\\n      poolCount: pool.length,\\r\\n      inBandCount: pool.length,\\r\\n      outOfBandUsed: 0,\\r\\n      warning: null,\\r\\n      ...describeSet(qids, stats, targetTrapTags),\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Rank, do not filter. The band decides preference order, never eligibility,\\r\\n  // so the drill is always the length it was meant to be:\\r\\n  //   qids.length === min(want, pool.length), at every level.\\r\\n  // An empty or short drill is impossible by construction rather than by\\r\\n  // fallback, which matters because a two-question drill reads as a bug to the\\r\\n  // student and there is no way for them to tell it was a levelling decision.\\r\\n  //\\r\\n  // Two optional signals ride on top, and each is confined to one job:\\r\\n  //   empiricalWeight decides MEMBERSHIP  (is this question really at level?)\\r\\n  //   trapWeight      decides ORDER       (which of the eligible ones first?)\\r\\n  // Keeping them apart is what makes each provable on its own, and what lets\\r\\n  // both default to off without a special case threaded through the algorithm.\\r\\n  //\\r\\n  const useEmpirical = empiricalWeight > 0 && stats != null && stats.size > 0;\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4d5673","wall_time_seconds":1.9619581,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":748,"output":"// The question bank and a drill\'s pool, for local Node tooling.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// Pulled out of report-drill-levels.mjs so the report, the qid resolver and\\r\\n// anything else agree by construction rather than by two people remembering\\r\\n// to edit both. Mirrors resolveGuidedDrillPool in src/lib/guidedDrills.ts.\\r\\n\\r\\nimport fs from \'node:fs\';\\r\\nimport path from \'node:path\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst BANK_DIR = path.resolve(\'public/data\');\\r\\nconst STATS = path.resolve(\'public/data/question-stats.json\');\\r\\nconst SEED = path.resolve(\'data/guided-drills/_seed.json\');\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Every LR question the local bank files know, keyed by qid. */\\r\\nexport function loadBank() {\\r\\n  const byQid = new Map();\\r\\n  for (const file of fs.readdirSync(BANK_DIR).filter((n) => /-LR\\\\.json$/.test(n))) {\\r\\n    let arr;\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      arr = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(BANK_DIR, file), \'utf8\'));\\r\\n    } catch {\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    if (!Array.isArray(arr)) continue;\\r\\n    for (const q of arr) {\\r\\n      byQid.set(`${q.practiceTest}-S${q.sectionNumber}-Q${q.questionNumber}`, q);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return byQid;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function loadSeed() {\\r\\n  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(SEED, \'utf8\'));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Cohort stats hydrated into the shape resolveLeveledDrillQids expects.\\r\\n * The sidecar\'s keys are abbreviated; this mirrors hydrate() in\\r\\n * src/lib/questionStats.ts.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function loadStats() {\\r\\n  if (!fs.existsSync(STATS)) return new Map();\\r\\n  const payload = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(STATS, \'utf8\'));\\r\\n  const map = new Map();\\r\\n  for (const [qid, raw] of Object.entries(payload.q ?? {})) {\\r\\n    map.set(qid, {\\r\\n      correctChoice: raw.c,\\r\\n      correctPct: raw.cp,\\r\\n      empiricalDifficulty: raw.ed,\\r\\n      topTrapLetter: raw.tl,\\r\\n      topTrapTag: raw.tt,\\r\\n      topTrapTagRaw: raw.ttr,\\r\\n      topTrapPct: raw.tp,\\r\\n      trapSpread: raw.ts,\\r\\n      choicePcts: raw.p,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return map;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Mirrors resolveGuidedDrillPool in src/lib/guidedDrills.ts. */\\r\\nexport function resolvePool(bankFilter, bank) {\\r\\n  const f = bankFilter ?? {};\\r\\n  const qtypes = new Set((f.qtypes || []).map((s) => s.toLowerCase()));\\r\\n  const features = new Set((f.features || []).map((s) => s.toLowerCase()));\\r\\n  const reasoning = new Set((f.reasoning_types || []).map((s) => s.toLowerCase()));\\r\\n  const dmin = f.difficulty_min ?? null;\\r\\n  const dmax = f.difficulty_max ?? null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const out = [];\\r\\n  for (const [qid, q] of bank) {\\r\\n    if (qtypes.size && !qtypes.has((q.questionType || \'\').toLowerCase())) continue;\\r\\n    if (features.size && !(q.questionFeatures || []).some((x) => features.has((x || \'\').toLowerCase())))\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    if (reasoning.size && !(q.reasoningType || []).some((x) => reasoning.has((x || \'\').toLowerCase())))\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    const d = q.questionDifficulty;\\r\\n    if (!Number.isFinite(d)) continue;\\r\\n    if (dmin != null && d < dmin) continue;\\r\\n    if (dmax != null && d > dmax) continue;\\r\\n    out.push({ qid, difficulty: d, type: q.questionType });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4b295c","wall_time_seconds":1.7635179,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":111,"output":"import fs from \'node:fs\';\\r\\nimport path from \'node:path\';\\r\\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \'node:url\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const vaultRoot = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \'../..\');\\r\\nconst sibling = path.join(path.dirname(vaultRoot), \'ai-os-projects\');\\r\\nexport const projectsRoot = fs.existsSync(sibling) ? sibling : path.join(vaultRoot, \'projects\');\\r\\nexport const projectPath = (...parts) => path.join(projectsRoot, ...parts);\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

The main slowdown was repeated setup: each assignment refreshed the checkout, started the runner, and signed in again. I also searched too broadly and opened a browser unnecessarily.

I’m adding a batch path that resolves the student once, checks all three existing drills before writing, then assigns and verifies them in one session. I’ll expose it through the existing local MCP and add a searchable drill catalog.

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"a22cbc","wall_time_seconds":1.4944652999999999,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2742,"output":"---\\r\\nname: writing-for-agents\\r\\ndescription: Writing documents for agents. Use when creating or editing skills, or modifying AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nReference for writing any document an agent consumes: a skill, an `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md`, a doc reached by a pointer. The packaging differs; the writing does not: the same levers make each one predictable, since the agent takes the same _process_ every run rather than producing the same output.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the document you\'re writing is a skill, read [`SKILL-MECHANICS.md`](SKILL-MECHANICS.md) for frontmatter, invocation choice, and router skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Context pointers\\r\\n\\r\\nA **context pointer** is a reference held in the agent\'s context that names some out-of-context material and encodes the condition for reaching it. A skill\'s description is one; a line in `AGENTS.md` naming a doc is the same object. The pointer\'s _wording_, not its target, decides when the agent reaches the material, and how reliably. A must-have target behind a weakly worded pointer is a variance bug: sharpen the wording first, and inline the material only if sharpening fails.\\r\\n\\r\\nA pointer does two jobs: state what the material is, and list the **branches** that should trigger reaching it (a branch is a distinct case the document handles, so different runs take different paths through it). Every word of an always-loaded pointer costs on every turn, so it earns even harder pruning than the body:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Front-load the leading word**: the pointer is where it does its triggering work.\\r\\n- **One trigger per branch.** Synonyms that rename a single branch are one branch written twice; collapse them and keep only genuinely distinct branches.\\r\\n- **Cut identity the body already carries.**\\r\\n\\r\\n## The two loads\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery document and pointer you add spends one of two budgets:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Context load** is the cost of always-loaded material on the agent\'s window: an `AGENTS.md` line, a skill description, anything sitting in context every turn, spending tokens and attention whether or not it fires.\\r\\n- **Cognitive load** is the cost on the human: which documents exist and when to reach for each. The human is the index. Not a cost to minimise: it is the price of human agency; spend it where human judgement matters, remove it where it does not.\\r\\n\\r\\nMaterial reached only through a pointer escapes context load at the price of the pointer\'s own line; material with no pointer at all rides entirely on cognitive load.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Information hierarchy\\r\\n\\r\\nA document is built from two content types: **steps** (the ordered actions the agent performs) and **reference** (definitions, rules, facts consulted on demand). The two mix freely: all steps (a recipe), all reference (a review\'s rules, this skill), or both. The core decision is where each piece sits on the **information hierarchy**, a ladder ranked by how immediately the agent needs the material:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **In-file step** is the primary tier: what the agent does, in order.\\r\\n2. **In-file reference** is consulted on demand. Often a legitimately flat peer-set (every rule of a review on one rung), which is a fine arrangement, not a smell.\\r\\n3. **Disclosed reference** is pushed out into a separate file, reached by a context pointer, loaded only when the pointer fires. Spans a sibling file in the same folder through fully external reference that lives anywhere and any document can point at.\\r\\n\\r\\nPush too little down and the top bloats; push too much and you hide material the agent actually needs. That tension is the whole decision.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Progressive disclosure** is the move down the ladder (out of the main file and behind a pointer) so the top stays legible. Not primarily a token optimisation: it is how the hierarchy is protected. Branching is the cleanest disclosure test: inline what every branch needs, and push behind a pointer what only some branches reach. When a document has steps, in-file reference that should be disclosed buries them and turns attending to them into a coin-flip: a variance lever, not just a legibility one.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Co-location** is the within-file companion: where the ladder decides _how far down_ a piece sits, co-location decides _what sits beside it_ once there. Keep a concept\'s definition, rules, and caveats under one heading rather than scattered, so reading one part brings its neighbours with it. The test: the document should read like documentation written for the agent. Grouped material reads that way; scattered material does not. (Distinct from duplication: that repeats one meaning in two places; scattering fragments one meaning across many.)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Sprawl** is the failure mode here: a document simply too long, even when every line is live and unique. Attention thins across the excess, and every extra line is one more to keep relevant. The cure is the ladder: disclose reference behind pointers, and split by branch or sequence so each path carries only what it needs.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps and completion criteria\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery step ends on a **completion criterion**, the condition that tells the agent the work is done. Two properties make it a lever:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Clarity**: can the agent tell done from not-done? A vague bound (\\"understanding reached\\") invites **premature completion**: ending the step before it is genuinely done, attention slipping to _being done_. The visible steps still ahead (the **post-completion steps**) supply the pull; the criterion\'s clarity is the resistance. Defend in order: **sharpen the bound first** (local and cheap); only if it is irreducibly fuzzy _and_ you observe the rush, hide the later steps by splitting the sequence. Hiding only works across a real context boundary (a hand-off or a subagent dispatch; an inline call leaves the later steps in context and clears nothing).\\r\\n- **Demand**: how much it requires. \\"Every modified model accounted for\\" forces thorough work where \\"produce a change list\\" does not. Demand drives **legwork** (the digging the agent does within the work, latent in the wording rather than written as its own step), and it is not step-bound: \\"every rule applied\\" binds a body of flat reference just as \\"every step done\\" binds a sequence, which is how an all-reference document still carries an exhaustiveness bar.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe strongest criteria are both checkable and exhaustive.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When to split\\r\\n\\r\\nSplitting one document into two spends one of the two loads, so split only when the cut earns it:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **By sequence**: split a run of steps where the post-completion steps tempt the agent to rush the one in front of it. Keeping them out of view drives more legwork on the current task. Beware the reverse: merging sequences exposes each step\'s later steps to what follows, inviting premature completion.\\r\\n- **By invocation**, skill-specific: see [`SKILL-MECHANICS.md`](SKILL-MECHANICS.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Leading words\\r\\n\\r\\nA **leading word** is a compact concept already living in the model\'s pretraining that the agent thinks with while running the document (_lesson_, _fog of war_, _tracer bullets_). Repeated as a token, never as a sentence, it accumulates a distributed definition and anchors a whole region of behaviour in the fewest tokens, by recruiting priors the model already holds. Coining your own works if you define it clearly, but a made-up word recruits no priors: you pay in definition tokens what a pretrained word gives free; reach for an existing word first.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt anchors twice. In the body, _execution_: the agent reaches for the same behaviour every time the word appears, and inside flat reference it focuses attention on a class of thing to look for. In a pointer, _invocation_: when the same word lives in your prompts, your docs, and your codebase, the agent links that shared language to the material and reaches it more reliably.\\r\\n\\r\\nHunt for opportunities to refactor with leading words. A triad spelled out at three sites, a pointer spending a sentence to gesture at one idea. Each is a passage begging to collapse into a single token:\\r\\n\\r\\n- \\"fast, deterministic, low-overhead\\" → _tight_ (a _tight_ loop).\\r\\n- \\"a loop you believe in\\" → _red_, turning a fuzzy gate into a binary observable state (the loop goes _red_ on the bug, or it doesn\'t).\\r\\n\\r\\nYou win twice: fewer tokens, and a sharper hook for the agent to hang its thinking on. Assume every document is carrying restatements that leading words retire. Go find them.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Negation** is the failure mode beside this lever: steering by prohibition drags the forbidden behaviour into context and makes it _more_ available, not less. _Don\'t think of an elephant_, and the elephant is all there is; the negation is a weak modifier the strongly-activated concept overruns, so the ban half-reads as an instruction to do the thing. Prompt the **positive**: state the target behaviour (\\"write one-line comments\\") so the banned one is never spoken. A prohibition earns its place only as a hard guardrail you cannot phrase positively; even then, pair it with the positive target so attention lands on what to do.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pruning\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep each meaning in a **single source of truth**: one authoritative place, so changing the behaviour is a one-place edit. **Duplication** (the same meaning in more than one place) costs maintenance and tokens, and inflates a meaning\'s prominence on the ladder past its real rank. (The accidental inverse of a leading word, which repeats a token on purpose, never the meaning.)\\r\\n- The **environment** is a source of truth too (`package.json` scripts, config files, the directory layout, `--help` output), and a document that restates it is a **cache**: a copy of a lookup, earning its load only when the lookup is expensive. Cache what the agent cannot find by looking: the unwritten convention, the reason behind a choice, the gotcha no config confesses. Leave the one-file, one-command lookups to the environment, where they cannot go stale.\\r\\n- Check every line for **relevance**: does it still bear on what the document does? A line loses relevance by never bearing on the task (mere exposition, or a branch that should be disclosed) or by going stale as the behaviour or world it describes changes. Shorter documents are easier to keep relevant. Without a pruning discipline the default fate is **sediment**: stale layers that settle because adding feels safe and removing feels risky, until you must core down through them to find what is still live.\\r\\n- Hunt **no-ops** sentence by sentence: an instruction the model already obeys by default pays load to say nothing. The test (does it change behaviour versus the default?) is model-relative, not reader-relative: two people disagreeing about a no-op disagree about the default, and settle it by running the document, not by debate. When a sentence fails, delete the whole sentence rather than trim words from it. The test also grades leading words: a word too weak to beat the default (_be thorough_ when the agent is already thorough-ish) is a no-op, and the fix is a stronger word (_relentless_), not a different technique.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"39f68d","wall_time_seconds":1.6863766999999998,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"cf3f35","wall_time_seconds":1.4584312,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1509,"output":"---\\r\\nname: skill-creator\\r\\ndescription: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill\'s description for better triggering accuracy.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Skill Creator\\r\\n\\r\\nA skill for creating new skills and iteratively improving them.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt a high level, the process of creating a skill goes like this:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Decide what you want the skill to do and roughly how it should do it\\r\\n- Write a draft of the skill\\r\\n- Create a few test prompts and run Codex-with-access-to-the-skill on them\\r\\n- Help the user evaluate the results both qualitatively and quantitatively\\r\\n  - While the runs happen in the background, draft some quantitative evals if there aren\'t any (if there are some, you can either use as is or modify if you feel something needs to change about them). Then explain them to the user (or if they already existed, explain the ones that already exist)\\r\\n  - Use the `eval-viewer/generate_review.py` script to show the user the results for them to look at, and also let them look at the quantitative metrics\\r\\n- Rewrite the skill based on feedback from the user\'s evaluation of the results (and also if there are any glaring flaws that become apparent from the quantitative benchmarks)\\r\\n- Repeat until you\'re satisfied\\r\\n- Expand the test set and try again at larger scale\\r\\n\\r\\nYour job when using this skill is to figure out where the user is in this process and then jump in and help them progress through these stages. So for instance, maybe they\'re like \\"I want to make a skill for X\\". You can help narrow down what they mean, write a draft, write the test cases, figure out how they want to evaluate, run all the prompts, and repeat.\\r\\n\\r\\nOn the other hand, maybe they already have a draft of the skill. In this case you can go straight to the eval/iterate part of the loop.\\r\\n\\r\\nOf course, you should always be flexible and if the user is like \\"I don\'t need to run a bunch of evaluations, just vibe with me\\", you can do that instead.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen after the skill is done (but again, the order is flexible), you can also run the skill description improver, which we have a whole separate script for, to optimize the triggering of the skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nCool? Cool.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Communicating with the user\\r\\n\\r\\nThe skill creator is liable to be used by people across a wide range of familiarity with coding jargon. If you haven\'t heard (and how could you, it\'s only very recently that it started), there\'s a trend now where the power of Codex is inspiring plumbers to open up their terminals, parents and grandparents to google \\"how to install npm\\". On the other hand, the bulk of users are probably fairly computer-literate.\\r\\n\\r\\nSo please pay attention to context cues to understand how to phrase your communication! In the default case, just to give you some idea:\\r\\n\\r\\n- \\"evaluation\\" and \\"benchmark\\" are borderline, but OK\\r\\n- for \\"JSON\\" and \\"assertion\\" you want to see serious cues from the user that they know what those things are before using them without explaining them\\r\\n\\r\\nIt\'s OK to briefly explain terms if you\'re in doubt, and feel free to clarify terms with a short definition if you\'re unsure if the user will get it.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Creating a skill\\r\\n\\r\\n### Capture Intent\\r\\n\\r\\nStart by understanding the user\'s intent. The current conversation might already contain a workflow the user wants to capture (e.g., they say \\"turn this into a skill\\"). If so, extract answers from the conversation history first — the tools used, the sequence of steps, corrections the user made, input/output formats observed. The user may need to fill the gaps, and should confirm before proceeding to the next step.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. What should this skill enable Codex to do?\\r\\n2. When should this skill trigger? (what user phrases/contexts)\\r\\n3. What\'s the expected output format?\\r\\n4. Should we set up test cases to verify the skill works? Skills with objectively verifiable outputs (file transforms, data extraction, code generation, fixed workflow steps) benefit from test cases. Skills with subjective outputs (writing style, art) often don\'t need them. Suggest the appropriate default based on the skill type, but let the user decide.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Interview and Research\\r\\n\\r\\nProactively ask questions about edge cases, input/output formats, example files, success criteria, and dependencies. Wait to write test prompts until you\'ve got this part ironed out.\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck available MCPs - if useful for research (searching docs, finding similar skills, looking up best practices), research in parallel via subagents if available, otherwise inline. Come prepared with context to reduce burden on the user.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Write the SKILL.md\\r\\n\\r\\nBased on the user interview, fill in these components:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **name**: Skill identifier\\r\\n- **description**: When to trigger, what it does. This is the primary triggering mechanism - include both what the skill does AND specific contexts for when to use it. All \\"when to use\\" info goes here, not in the body. Note: currently Codex has a tendency to \\"undertrigger\\" skills -- to not use them when they\'d be useful. To combat this, please make the skill descriptions a little bit \\"pushy\\". So for instance, instead of \\"How to build a simple fast dashboard to display internal Anthropic data.\\", you might write \\"How to build a simple fast dashboard to display internal Anthropic data. Make sure to use this skill whenever the user mentions dashboards, data visualization, internal metrics, or wants to display any kind of company data, even if they don\'t explicitly ask for a \'dashboard.\'\\"\\r\\n- **compatibility**: Required tools, dependencies (optional, rarely needed)\\r\\n- **the rest of the skill :)**\\r\\n\\r\\n### Skill Writing Guide\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Anatomy of a Skill\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nskill-name/\\r\\n├── SKILL.md (required)\\r\\n│   ├── YAML frontmatter (name, description required)\\r\\n│   └── Markdown instructions\\r\\n└── Bundled Resources (optional)\\r\\n"}'}]

No automatic write retries.\n+let guidedQueue = Promise.resolve();\n+async function guidedCommand(plan, dryRun) {\n+  const operation = guidedQueue.then(async () => {\n+    try {\n+      const { stdout } = await run(process.execPath, [guidedPlan, '--json', JSON.stringify(plan), ...(dryRun ? ['--dry-run'] : [])], {\n+        cwd: vault, windowsHide: true, maxBuffer: 4 * 1024 * 1024, timeout: 180000,\n+      });\n+      const result = JSON.parse(stdout.trim());\n+      return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(result) }], structuredContent: result };\n+    } catch (error) {\n+      return { isError: true, content: [{ type: 'text', text: String(error.stderr || error.message).trim() }] };\n+    }\n+  });\n+  guidedQueue = operation.then(() => undefined, () => undefined);\n+  return operation;\n+}\n+\n+const guidedAssignment = z.object({\n+  drill: z.string().min(2).describe('Exact published guided drill slug from search_guided_drills.'),\n+  schedule: z.string().regex(/^\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$/),\n+  due: z.string().regex(/^\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$/),\n+  level: z.enum(['beginner', 'intermediate', 'advanced']).nullable().optional(),\n+  count: z.number().int().min(1).max(50).optional().describe('Omit to retain the existing drill size and matching instruction card.'),\n+  max_difficulty: z.number().int().min(1).max(5).optional().describe('Hard preflight ceiling on every question that can be drawn at the requested level.'),\n+});\n+const guidedSchema = {\n+  id: z.string().optional(),\n+  student: z.string().min(2).describe('One exact student name or email.'),\n+  assignments: z.array(guidedAssignment).min(1).max(14),\n+};\n+\n+server.registerTool('search_guided_drills', {\n+  title: 'Find matching LSAT U guided drills',\n+  description: 'Search the mapped guided drill catalog by title, question type, reasoning, feature, or slug. Returns compact difficulty/pool metadata and snapshot time; assignment preflight rechecks live data.',\n+  inputSchema: { query: z.string().default(''), max_difficulty: z.number().int().min(1).max(5).optional(), limit: z.number().int().min(1).max(30).default(12) },\n+  annotations: { readOnlyHint: true },\n+}, ({ query, max_difficulty, limit }) => {\n+  const catalog = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(vault, 'references/lsatu-guided-drill-catalog.json'), 'utf8'));\n+  const words = query.toLowerCase().trim().split(/\\s+/).filter(Boolean);\n+  const matching = catalog.drills.filter(d => d.is_published && words.every(word => JSON.stringify([d.slug, d.title, d.bank_filter, d.reasoning_types]).toLowerCase().includes(word)) && (max_difficulty == null || (d.pool_summary.difficulty_max != null && d.pool_summary.difficulty_max <= max_difficulty)));\n+  const result = { snapshot: catalog.meta.generated_at, total_matches: matching.length, drills: matching.slice(0, limit).map(d => ({ slug: d.slug, title: d.title, count: d.question_count, minutes: d.estimated_minutes, catalog_level: d.level, native_band: [d.bank_filter.difficulty_min, d.bank_filter.difficulty_max], pool: d.pool_summary, bank_filter: d.bank_filter })) };\n+  return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(result) }], structuredContent: result };\n+});\n+\n+server.registerTool('preview_guided_drill_batch', {\n+  title: 'Preflight a guided drill schedule',\n+  description: 'Read-only live preflight for one student and up to 14 guided assignments.
